# Round-7 UX/Design Research — External Best Practices

> 25 Sonnet agents (web). Competitive analysis (QRadar/Splunk/Sentinel/Chronicle/Elastic/CrowdStrike/Cortex) + pattern research for the Command Center, Noise-Reduction funnel, case-story view, provenance labeling, motion, and a11y.


## IBM QRadar SIEM — dashboard, offense/incident management, and analyst-workflow UX patterns (for the Round-7 Command Center / Noise-Reduction funnel / Case Story overhaul)


**Key findings:**
- Offense list (Offenses tab): a filterable/sortable table with a dedicated MAGNITUDE column rendered as a 10-segment horizontal bar, color-banded green (1-3 low) / yellow (4-7 medium) / red (8-10 high) and filled proportionally — analysts scan bar shape, not a bare number, to triage at a glance. Filters: Magnitude, Severity, Assigned To, Status, Start Time, Offense Type, Log Source Name/Type, Destination Network, Local Destination Addresses, Source Addresses, Rules, Follow Up, Protected. Right-click on Status/Type/Source IP/Destination IP instantly adds an IS / IS NOT filter.
- Magnitude is explicitly a COMPOSITE the UI always shows broken down, never just a single number: Relevance (asset/network weight — how much this matters to you), Severity (threat level relative to the target's preparedness), Credibility (how trustworthy the reporting log source is, refined by corroboration across sources). Docs stress severity/credibility/relevance are computed off the event's CATEGORY (not the raw event); event/flow count, log-source count, offense age, and asset vulnerabilities also feed the final 0-10 score. This 'show the sub-scores, not just the composite' pattern is the single most reusable idea for us.
- Offense Summary window (opened by double-clicking an offense) has 3 zones: (1) top metrics row — Magnitude/Relevance/Severity/Credibility + status icon; (2) 'Offense Information' FACT panel — Description (literally shows 'Preceded by <rule>' when this offense absorbed an earlier chained one), Offense Type, clickable Event/Flow counts, Source/Destination IP(s), Start time, Duration, Network(s); (3) a bottom 'Top Contributors' rollup that is an explicit capped-at-5 summarization: Top 5 Source IPs, Top 5 Destination IPs (with a Chained yes/no column + aggregate CVSS magnitude), Top 5 Log Sources, Top 5 Users, Top 5 Categories, Last 10 Events, Last 10 Flows, Last 5 Notes, Last 5 Search Results, and an 'Attack Path' network diagram (if Risk Manager installed). This is IBM's own noise-reduction/'what's driving this case' summarization pattern.
- IBM's docs draw an explicit 3-way separation of content types on the offense screen: (a) FACTS — Offense Information + Top-5 contributor lists + Last-10 events/flows, all pulled straight from raw log data, read-only, drill-through to source records; (b) HUMAN INPUT — Notes, which the docs state are explicitly 'non-editable/non-deletable' once added (append-only, audit-safe); (c) SYSTEM/AI-DERIVED — 'Annotations', which IBM's own documentation literally defines as 'system-generated insights explaining threat assessment,' shown as their own distinct list separate from both raw events and human notes. This is a directly reusable 3-lane precedent (facts / human thread / machine reasoning) for our case story view.
- Offense chaining/correlation: offenses are grouped by an admin-chosen 'offense index field' (commonly source IP, but can be username/dest IP/etc.); while an offense is active, any new matching event with the same index value joins it instead of spawning a duplicate. The Offense Source Summary pane explicitly surfaces a 'Chained: Yes/No' field, and the Description field appends the names of every rule that subsequently matched — giving analysts a one-line provenance/lineage trail of what got folded into the case, not just an opaque count.
- QRadar Pulse (the modern dashboard app): per-user private 'workspaces' containing 'dashboards' (containers) of 'widgets' (one AQL/offense-data query + one chart, reusable/duplicable across dashboards). Curated, admin-published 'dashboard templates' can be installed org-wide with auto version updates. Dashboards can be shared as read-only links (drill-down still works, but recipients only ever see data their own privileges allow — access control is enforced per-viewer, not per-dashboard), exported/imported as JSON, and switched into a themed 'SOC wall display' mode for the ops floor.
- IBM's QRadar 7.5.0 UP13 release added an infographic-style visual summary (timeline + magnitude ranking + asset grouping) directly onto the Offenses tab to speed triage — praised for surfacing patterns/peaks and steering attention to high-impact offenses at a glance, but drew real user backlash: it crowded table columns on smaller screens, shipped with no native per-user/per-role toggle to hide or resize it, and users resorted to unsupported CSS hacks to suppress it before IBM added real controls.
- IBM QRadar SOAR (the case-management tier offenses escalate into) organizes an incident as tabs: Case Overview, Threat Timeline, Attack Graph, Artifacts, Tasks — plus fully admin-configurable 'incident layouts' that can hide default tabs, add custom tabs, and show/hide whole sections conditionally based on incident type/severity. This 'layout is data-driven and conditional, not one fixed template' pattern is worth mirroring for our case detail view across different case/verdict types.
- Independent reviews (Comparitech) and Gartner Peer Insights call out two recurring UX weaknesses worth treating as anti-patterns: a black-background/white-or-gray-text scheme that is 'great for making colored graphs stand out but makes text difficult to read' and 'a strain on the eyes' on extended use, and repeated complaints about slow filter/search response time and I/O lag while loading offense/event filters during active investigation.

**Concrete recommendations for us:**
- Build a reusable `<MagnitudeBar value={0-10} bands={[3,7]} />` primitive (10 discrete cells, green/yellow/red fill by threshold, like QRadar's) for our risk/severity chips in the Cases table and Command Center KPI row — use it as a compact row-level sparkbar, distinct from the full radial RiskGauge already used on hero/case-detail; never show a bare risk number with no visual band.
- On every one of our 4 triage chips (risk/severity/impact/priority), add a hover-card or expand-on-click breakdown that shows the deterministic sub-factors composing it — mirroring QRadar always showing Relevance+Severity+Credibility next to Magnitude. Wire this to our existing `GET /api/cases/{id}/rationale` (already returns decision_rationale/evidence/mitre) so the breakdown is data we already compute, just needs a UI affordance (a `TriageChipBreakdown` popover) — don't ship a composite score with no drill-in.
- Add a capped 'Top Contributors' panel to the Command Center and to each case — Top-5 rule/signature, Top-5 source entity (ip/host/user), Top-5 destination/asset, Top-5 log source/connector, Top-5 MITRE tactic — each a small ranked list with counts and a 'view all' link. This directly answers 'what's driving the noise right now' the way QRadar's Offense Summary bottom section does, without opening every case.
- Redesign the case-story view (CaseDetail) around QRadar's literal 3-lane precedent: a FACTS rail sourced straight from OCSF events/enrichment (read-only, each item drills to raw evidence, rendered as plain text/code per non-negotiable #9), a HUMAN THREAD rail (our existing case_thread/case_activity — keep it append-only/non-editable once posted, matching QRadar's 'notes are non-editable/non-deletable' audit-safety pattern), and an AI ASSESSMENT rail (verdict/confidence/reasoning from the investigator) — give the AI rail a persistent visual marker (icon + distinct background tint, not just a label) since QRadar's own docs single out 'Annotations' as system-generated insight, separate from both raw facts and human notes. Do NOT interleave all three into one flat chronological feed.
- For the case-story provenance line, add a one-line lineage/chain summary at the top of CaseDetail modeled on QRadar's 'Preceded by <rule>' description text and 'Chained: Yes/No' field — e.g. 'This case absorbed 3 prior matches from Rule X and Rule Y across 2 sources' — sourced from our correlation/campaign engine (engine/campaigns.py, engine/correlation) so grouping is explainable, not a silent black box.
- Design the 'Noise Reduction' alerts→cases funnel as a literal waterfall/Sankey with QRadar's own noise-reduction vocabulary and structure: Raw events/flows ingested → deduplicated by cluster signature → correlated into cases → auto-closed as false-positive → escalated to human. Each stage should show a clickable count (QRadar makes its Event/Flow counts on the offense summary directly clickable through to the underlying records) that filters the Cases list to that stage — don't just show static numbers.
- Any new rich visual panel we add to a LIST view (e.g. an infographic hero row on the Cases page or a Command Center widget) MUST ship with a per-user show/hide toggle from day one and must degrade gracefully at common laptop widths (test ~1280–1440px, not just wide monitors) — this directly avoids the QRadar 7.5 UP13 backlash (crowded columns, no native toggle, users hacking CSS to hide it). Wire the toggle through our existing per-user `UserPrefsStore`/saved-views mechanism (already built in Round 2 W7b) rather than inventing a new prefs path.
- Extend our existing per-user custom dashboards (Round 5 G7) with a 'wall/TV mode' — fullscreen, larger fonts, chrome stripped, auto-rotating between saved dashboards — and a 'share read-only link' mode where the recipient always sees data clipped to their own RBAC scope (mirrors QRadar Pulse's shareable dashboard links + per-viewer privilege enforcement), reusing our existing role/permission checks rather than a new sharing-permission model.
- Make our case-detail tab layout data-driven/conditional per case type or verdict (e.g. hide the MITRE/campaign tabs for a case with no correlation, show an escalation-specific tab only for ESCALATED cases) — mirrors QRadar SOAR's admin-configurable, condition-aware incident layouts — rather than one fixed tab set for every case.
- Explicitly stress-test our dark-theme risk/severity/verdict color tokens for text-on-dark contrast at small sizes (chip labels, table cells) beyond the WCAG-AA pass already claimed for Round 5 G1 — QRadar's own black-background/white-text scheme is called out repeatedly as eye strain in extended SOC shifts; verify with real 8-12hr-shift-style scrutiny, not just an automated contrast checker on isolated swatches.
- Ensure the Cases table and Command Center filters are server-side filtered/paginated with cached queries (React Query) rather than client-side full recompute on every filter change — directly avoids the repeated 'slow filter/search response, I/O lag while loading offense filters' complaints found in third-party QRadar reviews.

**Pitfalls to avoid:**
- Don't ship a single opaque composite score (risk/priority) with no way to see what's driving it — QRadar analysts specifically rely on seeing Relevance/Severity/Credibility broken out next to Magnitude; a case could score high purely because of one factor (e.g. a highly weighted asset) while looking deceptively 'severe' overall.
- Don't add a rich visual/infographic panel to a list view without a per-user/per-role hide toggle and without testing it at normal laptop widths — IBM's own 7.5 UP13 infographic panel drew a documented user backlash (crowded columns, no native toggle, workaround CSS hacks) for exactly this omission.
- Don't silently auto-merge/correlate cases by a single shared field (e.g. source IP) without making the grouping reasoning inspectable and reversible — QRadar's offense-chaining-by-index-field is a known operational gotcha when the index field is reused across unrelated contexts (e.g. shared/NAT'd IPs), silently lumping unrelated incidents into one case; always expose 'why grouped' and provide an un-merge/split path.
- Don't let 'system-generated insight' (AI annotations/verdict) visually blend into either the raw-fact stream or the human-notes thread — IBM's docs single out annotations as their own distinct, clearly-labeled category; mixing AI reasoning into a flat chronological feed with human comments erodes trust and blurs non-negotiable #3 (verdict vs. deterministic decision must stay visibly separate).
- Don't make human-authored notes/comments silently editable after posting — QRadar notes are explicitly non-editable/non-deletable for audit integrity; if we allow edits, at minimum keep full edit history (already consistent with our non-negotiable #2 append-only audit).
- Don't invert a light theme into 'black background + light text' as your only dark-mode strategy and call it done — that exact scheme is called out by name in independent QRadar reviews as hard to read and eye-straining over long sessions; validate actual in-context legibility of small chip/table text, not just headline contrast ratios.
- Don't let filter/search interactions block or lag the UI thread — recurring, specific complaints about QRadar's filter/search responsiveness during active investigation show that performance IS a UX feature for SOC tools where speed-to-decision is the whole point; a fast, correct interface beats a richer but sluggish one.
- Don't force analysts to leave the primary triage screen to see 'what's driving this alert volume' — QRadar's Top-5-contributors rollup exists specifically so the noise-reduction story is visible without opening individual offenses; if our Command Center only shows a raw case count with no contributor breakdown, we've under-delivered relative to this 2010s-era baseline.

**Sources:**

## Splunk Enterprise Security — Security Posture dashboard + Incident Review (now "Mission Control" analyst queue) UX: KPI layout, drill-down, and notable-event presentation


**Key findings:**
- Security Posture dashboard = exactly 5 panel types, all scoped to a fixed 24h window with 48h trend baseline: (1) Key Indicators — a row of KPI tiles (Splunk caps at 7 per row, multiple rows allowed) showing findings-by-security-domain counts; (2) Findings by Urgency; (3) Findings over Time (stacked timeline by domain); (4) Top Findings (rule name + count + inline sparkline); (5) Top Findings Sources (src field + total count + per-correlation-rule count + domain count + sparkline). Source: https://help.splunk.com/en/splunk-enterprise-security-8/user-guide/8.5/analytics/security-posture-dashboard
- Every KPI tile has exactly 4 elements: a big value number (clickable -> opens the raw underlying search/events), a trend AMOUNT (delta number), a trend ARROW (direction+color coded), and a threshold-driven color state (above threshold=red, below=green, no threshold set=neutral black). This is the entire visual grammar — no extra chrome. Source: https://help.splunk.com/en/splunk-enterprise-security-7/user-guide/7.2/dashboard-overview/key-indicators-in-splunk-enterprise-security
- EVERY panel on the Security Posture dashboard drills down into the SAME destination: Mission Control's analyst queue, pre-filtered by whatever was clicked (urgency level, domain+timeframe, rule name, or source). One consistent drill-down target, not a maze of different dashboards.
- Urgency is a fully deterministic, transparent 2-input lookup: urgency = f(severity, priority) where severity (6 levels: unknown/informational/low/medium/high/critical) comes from the correlation search/rule, and priority (5 levels: unknown/low/medium/high/critical) comes from the asset OR identity record (if both are set, the HIGHER one wins). This is rendered/configurable as an editable matrix/lookup table (Content Management), not a hidden black-box score. Source: https://help.splunk.com/en/splunk-enterprise-security-7/user-guide/7.3/incident-review/how-urgency-is-assigned-to-notable-events-in-splunk-enterprise-security and https://help.splunk.com/en/splunk-enterprise-security-8/administer/8.2/findings/configure-the-urgency-for-findings-in-splunk-enterprise-security
- Incident Review / analyst queue table shows: severity/urgency (informational/low/medium/high/critical), status (New/In Progress/Pending/Resolved/Closed), owner, security domain (Access/Audit/Endpoint/Identity/Network/Threat) — plus a companion 'Charts' view offering FOUR pie charts (by urgency, by status, by owner, by domain) and a zoomable event timeline, all as alternate views of the same underlying notable set (not separate pages). Source: https://help.splunk.com/en/splunk-enterprise-security-7/user-guide/7.3/incident-review/overview-of-incident-review-in-splunk-enterprise-security
- Per-notable investigation ('embedded workbench') separates data into clearly distinct sections rather than one blended feed: History (audit trail of analyst actions) · Related Investigations (links) · the correlation-search definition itself (config, read-only) · Contributing Events (the raw events that triggered it) · Risk Assessment (computed scores for the involved assets/identities, with a drill to a dedicated Risk Analysis dashboard) · Adaptive Response Log (automated actions already taken) · Next Steps (recommended, not automatic). Source: https://help.splunk.com/en/splunk-enterprise-security-7/user-guide/7.2/incident-review/investigate-a-notable-on-incident-review-in-splunk-enterprise-security and https://help.splunk.com/en/splunk-enterprise-security-7/administer/7.3/incident-review-and-investigations/investigate-notables-using-drilldown-searches-and-dashboards-in-splunk-enterprise-security
- Actions available directly on a notable in the queue (no page nav required): Run Adaptive Response Actions, Share (generates a short shareable ID/link), Add Event(s) to Investigation (single or bulk-select), Analyze Risk (drill to Risk Analysis dashboard), Suppress Notable Events (field-matched, with an optional expiration date — NOT a permanent silent kill), field-level pivots (Asset Investigator / Domain Dossier), and a per-notable 'notify me of related events' bell. Source: https://help.splunk.com/en/splunk-enterprise-security-7/user-guide/7.2/incident-review/take-action-on-a-notable-on-incident-review-in-splunk-enterprise-security
- Risk-Based Alerting (RBA) is Splunk's real noise-reduction funnel and the direct analog of an alerts->cases pipeline: narrow 'risk rules' (SPL + risk annotation + a risk-analysis adaptive-response action) write low-noise 'risk events' into a single Risk Index, each tagged to a 'risk object' (the asset/identity) and scored via 'risk factors' (org-defined multipliers, e.g., privileged user or internet-facing server bumps the score). A separate 'risk incident rule' sums all risk-index scores for a given risk object over a rolling window; only when the AGGREGATE crosses a threshold (Splunk's own example: 5+ risk events from different rules/MITRE techniques/data sources on one entity) does it mint ONE 'risk notable' that tells a consolidated story, instead of alerting on every individual weak signal. Source: https://help.splunk.com/en/splunk-enterprise-security-7/risk-based-alerting/7.3/introduction/how-risk-based-alerting-works-in-splunk-enterprise-security
- Splunk publishes a concrete, citable noise-reduction number for RBA: teams typically see a 50%-90% reduction in alert volume, with the surviving alerts being higher-fidelity and faster to investigate — the exact kind of before/after stat worth putting on a funnel infographic. Source: https://sp6.io/splunk-services/risk-based-alerting/ and https://lantern.splunk.com/Security_Use_Cases/Threat_Investigation/Implementing_risk-based_alerting
- Splunk Enterprise Security 8's 2025 rebrand ('Mission Control') explicitly retired 'Incident Review' -> 'Analyst Queue' and 'notable event' -> 'finding', and moved from full-page drilldown navigation to an in-place SIDE PANEL you open per finding (Assign to me -> change status -> Save -> Start investigation -> tabs for events/fields/notes/files/response-plan) — a design change Splunk's own docs frame as reducing the number of open tabs and giving 'a simplified drill-down experience.' Source: https://help.splunk.com/en/splunk-enterprise-security-8/user-guide/8.0/mission-control/overview-of-mission-control-in-splunk-enterprise-security and https://help.splunk.com/en/splunk-enterprise-security-8/administer/8.3/mission-control/manage-analyst-workflows-using-the-analyst-queue-in-splunk-enterprise-security

**Concrete recommendations for us:**
- Command Center KPI row: cap it at 6-7 KpiTiles per row (Splunk's own limit) and give every tile the exact 4-part anatomy Splunk uses — big number, delta amount, directional arrow, threshold color — but pair color with a SEMANTIC_ICON (we already do this per Round-5 G9; Splunk relies on color-only red/green which is a WCAG anti-pattern, so this is a real point of superiority to keep, not remove). Make the big number itself clickable straight into a pre-filtered CaseList (`webui/src/soc/components/KpiTile` + `DataTable`), exactly mirroring Splunk's 'click the value -> raw underlying events' pattern.
- Add two panels to the dashboard that we're missing vs. Splunk's Security Posture layout: a 'Top Detection Rules' panel (signature/rule name + open-case count + a small sparkline over the selected window, driven by `engine/signatures.py`) and a 'Top Offending Entities' panel (host/user/ip + count + how many distinct signatures fired + a sparkline, driven by `engine/campaigns.py`/`engine/threat_context.py` shared-entity data) — both clickable straight into the case queue filtered on that rule/entity.
- Make time-range control singular and shared: bind every dashboard panel to the ONE `TimeRangePicker` already in `webui/src/soc/components` (Round 6 shipped this for Overview) — do not let any panel silently default to a different implicit window than its siblings. If a panel legitimately needs a longer lookback for its sparkline/trend baseline (Splunk quietly uses 48h for KPI trend vs 24h for the main view), label it explicitly in the panel header (e.g. 'trend vs. prior 48h') rather than leaving it implicit — this exact ambiguity is a documented Splunk community confusion point.
- For the Noise-Reduction funnel infographic: model it directly on Splunk's RBA mental pipeline but populate it with OUR real numbers from `engine/metrics.py`/`engine/case_manager.py`: Stage 1 'Raw events ingested' (OCSF volume) -> Stage 2 'Correlated into clusters' (post `engine/correlation.py`) -> Stage 3 'Risk-scored candidates' (post `engine/risk.py` + baseline/anomaly signal) -> Stage 4 'Cases opened for triage' (post cost_gate/router/investigator) -> Stage 5 split into 'Auto-closed FP' / 'Escalated TP' / 'Needs human' (post the deterministic `decide()`). Render as a horizontal shrinking-width funnel (fits the wide `PageContainer`) with a headline reduction percentage computed live and phrased the way Splunk phrases its own RBA marketing stat ('be able to state your own X% reduction, updated live, not a static claim').
- Make every funnel stage clickable into the case queue filtered to that stage (same 'one consistent drill-down destination' discipline Splunk uses on its Security Posture dashboard) — do not send different stages to different pages.
- For the case 'story' view, adopt Splunk's investigate-a-notable section taxonomy almost verbatim but map it onto our own trust boundaries: an 'Evidence' zone = raw OCSF Contributing-Events table (read-only, monospace/table rendering, explicitly badged as untrusted per non-negotiable #9 — Splunk has no equivalent trust badge since it has no LLM in the loop, so this is a genuine differentiator to lean into, not soften); an 'AI Assessment' zone = verdict/confidence/reasoning (already the TraceTimeline 'Why' tab); a 'Deterministic Decision' zone that renders our priority/verdict math as an explicit visible chain the way Splunk renders 'Severity: High x Priority: Critical -> Urgency: Critical' — i.e. show 'Severity: High x Impact: Critical -> Priority: Critical' as a literal breadcrumb/small table next to the priority chip in `engine/priority.py`'s output, not just a colored badge with no visible arithmetic; and an 'Actions Taken' zone = the ActionType audit log, kept separate from evidence (mirrors Splunk's separate 'Adaptive Response Log').
- Borrow Splunk's 'Related Investigations' / campaign-linking pattern: surface 'Part of Campaign: <name> (N related cases)' directly in the case story header when `engine/campaigns.py` has linked the case, with a one-click pivot to the campaign view — same as Splunk's risk notable surfacing its contributing risk events and related notables inline.
- Borrow the Suppress-with-expiration action (not a permanent silent delete): add a 'Suppress similar for N days' case action that is HITL-proposal-gated (never a silent auto-close, keeping non-negotiable #3 intact) and that feeds into the existing `engine/threshold_tuner.py`/manual-suppression path, rather than only offering per-case close.
- Add a lightweight, clearly-labeled 'Suggested next steps' block to the case story view powered by the existing playbook-recommendation engine, mirroring Splunk's 'Next Steps' panel on notable investigation — label it as a RECOMMENDATION (not an executed action), consistent with non-negotiable #3.
- For the queue itself, add a quick-view side Sheet (we already have `ui/sheet`) triggered from a DataTable row so an analyst can triage (change status, add a note, see the verdict) without leaving the case list page — this mirrors ES8's explicit move away from full-page drilldown navigation toward an in-place side panel specifically to cut down on open tabs/lost context; reserve full CaseDetail navigation for the deep-dive.
- When rendering our own priority/urgency-style chip, avoid Splunk's documented failure mode: if one input to a multi-factor score (asset priority in Splunk's case; e.g. our impact/asset-criticality input) is unset/default, do NOT let it silently drag the combined value down with no explanation — render '(criticality not set — defaulting to Medium)' inline so an analyst never sees a rule set to 'critical' silently show as a low-priority case with no visible cause (this exact confusion is a named Splunk community complaint: correlation-rule severity=high rendering as urgency=informational because asset priority was unset).

**Pitfalls to avoid:**
- Hidden/mismatched time windows across panels on the same dashboard (Splunk's Security Posture mixes an implicit 24h main view with a 48h KPI-trend baseline with no visible label) — causes analysts to see inconsistent counts across panels and lose trust in the numbers; always make the window explicit and, by default, shared.
- Color-only threshold signaling on KPI tiles (red/green with no icon/text) — a known accessibility anti-pattern in Splunk's key-indicator design; don't replicate it, keep pairing color with an icon/label as we already do.
- Opaque, additive-only scoring with no visible arithmetic breeds distrust: Splunk's urgency formula IS actually simple and documented (severity x max(asset,identity priority) via an editable lookup), but when the UI just shows a final urgency badge with no visible inputs, analysts get confused by cases like a 'high' severity rule producing an 'informational' urgency because an unset asset-priority field silently won the lookup. Never show a derived severity/priority/risk value without a way to see WHY (its inputs) one click away.
- Fragmenting investigation across many separate full-page dashboards/tabs — Splunk's own ES8 redesign explicitly moved away from this toward an in-place side-panel workbench; don't build a case view that forces navigating to N different pages (asset dossier page, risk analysis page, drilldown search page, timeline page) to answer one triage question — consolidate into tabs/sections within one view.
- Suppression as a silent permanent kill switch with no expiry and no audit — Splunk's own Suppress action requires field-match criteria AND supports an optional expiration date specifically so noise-reduction doesn't quietly blind the team forever; any suppression/auto-tuning feature must be time-bounded, reversible, and audited (this is already how our `engine/threshold_tuner.py` HITL-proposal path is designed — keep it that way, don't add a 'suppress forever' shortcut).
- Treating the funnel/noise-reduction story as a one-time marketing number instead of a live, per-tenant, drill-downable metric — Splunk's 50-90% RBA reduction figure is compelling precisely because it's real and measured; a static illustrative funnel graphic with made-up proportions will read as less credible than one wired to `engine/metrics.py` live counts with every stage clickable.
- Overloading a single notable/case row with too many inline actions (Splunk's Actions column expands into 7+ menu items) — keep the primary CTA singular in the queue row (view/triage) and push secondary actions (suppress, share, add-to-investigation, run adaptive-response) into an overflow/kebab menu or the side-panel quick view, per Round-4's own 'single primary CTA' lesson already applied to CaseDetail.

**Sources:**
- https://help.splunk.com/en/splunk-enterprise-security-8/user-guide/8.5/analytics/security-posture-dashboard
- https://help.splunk.com/en/splunk-enterprise-security-7/user-guide/7.2/dashboard-overview/key-indicators-in-splunk-enterprise-security

## Microsoft Sentinel — dashboards/workbooks + incident investigation graph/UX (layout, chart choices, incident-story presentation) for the Round-7 TLSOC UI overhaul


**Key findings:**
- Incident details page uses a fixed 3-zone layout: a persistent LEFT panel (severity/status/owner controls + collapsed 'Evidence' links to Events/Alerts/Bookmarks + a first-4-entities preview, collapsible to a thin rail that still shows owner/status/severity), a CENTER tabbed area (Overview vs Entities tabs) holding the incident timeline + 'Similar incidents' + 'Entities' widgets, and a RIGHT column 'Top insights' widget of curated analytical/ML queries. Facts (timeline/entities/raw evidence) live left+center; analytical/derived content is spatially isolated on the right — the fact/derived split is done by SCREEN POSITION, not just color.
- The incident timeline widget is strictly 'alerts + hunting bookmarks in chronological order.' Clicking an item shows full alert detail (severity, status, rule, product, entities, MITRE tactics, System Alert ID) with a link that opens the underlying KQL query + raw events in an in-context 'Logs' slide-over panel — analysts never leave the incident page to see raw evidence.
- Entity drill-down is a 3-card side panel: Info (identity facts — SID, geolocation, org info, pulled straight from source systems), Timeline (chronological alerts/bookmarks/anomalies/activities for that entity, including ones NOT in this incident — shown de-emphasized with a dotted/grayed severity band and a '+' affordance to pull them in), and Insights (curated expert/ML queries, e.g. UEBA anomalies, TI matches, watchlist hits). Info+Timeline = fact; Insights = a separate, visually distinct card = derived/analytical — the same fact-vs-derived split is repeated at the entity level, not just the incident level.
- 'Similar incidents' widget shows a hover popover ('Similarity reason') explaining WHY an incident is similar (shared entity, rule name, or details) — a cheap, in-place explanation pattern with zero navigation cost, useful for a noise-reduction/duplicate-case UI.
- Security Copilot's incident summary is rendered as ONE explicit, separately-labeled card at the very TOP of the incident pane (before the human-authored description), collapsed by default behind 'Show more.' Its structured content is: attack start time+entity, a narrative timeline of how the attack unfolded, assets involved, IOCs, named threat actor, user/device risk+criticality, watchlist matches. Critically, every entity/file/IP/URL named in the summary text is a clickable citation that jumps to that entity's own fact page — the AI narrative visually cites back to the evidence it drew from rather than floating free-text.
- Defender Copilot's 'guided response' cards (Triage/Containment/Investigation/Remediation) each state: the recommended action, the target entity, WHY it's recommended (rationale), and whether it was ALREADY auto-executed by automation (past-tense, distinctly styled) vs is a pending proposal (actionable, with an explicit thumbs-up/down feedback icon on every card and permission-gated buttons shown grayed-out rather than hidden).
- The classic Investigation Graph is a node-link diagram where hovering an entity reveals a fly-out of predefined 'exploration queries' (e.g. 'related alerts', 'logged on to'); selecting one adds new nodes back into the graph connected by DOTTED edges (vs solid edges for original evidence) — a visual convention distinguishing directly-evidenced relationships from analyst-pulled context. A synced timeline sits alongside/below the graph; hovering a time point highlights what existed on the graph at that moment. Microsoft's own docs admit 'the downside of the graph is that you end up having to switch contexts a great deal more' — so the graph is an OPT-IN secondary view reached via an 'Investigate' button, never the default landing tab (default landing is the flat Overview tab).
- The newer (2026 GA) 'Sentinel graph' / custom graphs feature computes 'blast radius' from a compromised entity to critical assets, using a Graph Query Language (GQL) with incremental click-to-expand traversal to the next hop, rather than rendering the whole entity graph at once — good precedent for keeping a case's relationship graph small and demand-driven.
- The out-of-the-box 'Security operations efficiency' workbook is the canonical SOC dashboard: incidents-created-over-time (area/line), incidents by closing classification/severity/owner/status/product/tactic (stacked bars + grids), mean time to triage (MTTT) and mean time to closure (MTTC) reported BOTH as a mean AND as 5th/50th/90th/99th percentile tables (percentiles catch SLA-breaching outliers a mean hides), a per-owner MTTT/MTTC breakdown specifically framed as revealing 'which analysts are fastest and which need coaching', plus recent-activities and recent-closing-classification feeds.
- Workbook visual vocabulary is deliberately small and reused everywhere: KPI 'tiles' (one big number), grids (sortable/filterable tables, often with a hyperlink column that deep-links straight into the incident page — 'dashboard as launchpad'), time-series/area charts with 'time brushing' (click-drag a time range on the chart to scope every tile/grid below it), donut/stacked-bar for categorical breakdowns (classification/severity/tactic), and percentile tables for latency distributions.
- Microsoft explicitly recommends building DIFFERENT workbook variants per persona (SOC manager vs. analyst vs. asset/network owner) and per review cadence (hourly vs. daily), rather than one universal dashboard serving everyone.
- Auto-refresh on workbooks defaults OFF, supports 5-minute–1-day intervals, and is automatically turned back off whenever the workbook is closed/reopened — refresh is opt-in and re-confirmed, not a background default.
- Microsoft ran the legacy vs. new incident experience as a user-togglable A/B for an extended migration period ('toggle on the new experience at the top-right of the page') rather than a hard cutover of a page analysts live in daily.
- MITRE ATT&CK tactics/techniques are surfaced as plain badges/tags on alerts and rolled up into a distribution chart in the efficiency workbook — treated as ordinary deterministic fact (from rule-to-technique mapping), not visually flagged as 'AI-derived' even though it's an inference layer — provenance (deterministic code vs. LLM judgment), not 'was an algorithm involved', is what should decide fact-vs-assessment styling.

**Concrete recommendations for us:**
- Security Command Center dashboard: extend the existing per-role default Dashboard layouts (Round 6) with Sentinel-style percentile KPI tiles — every latency KpiTile (MTTA/MTTR/dwell, already computed in engine/metrics.py) should render P50 AND P90 (add P99 if cheap) side-by-side as a small 3-number cluster or mini distribution bar, not a single mean, so SLA-breaching outliers are visible without opening Metrics.
- Add a 'Time to triage/resolve by analyst' DataTable (sourced from Case.status_history + assignee) to the Metrics/Overview page, sorted by MTTA descending — this is a direct port of Sentinel's per-owner coaching table and is cheap to build from data we already track.
- Implement true time-brushing on the incident-volume/trend chart on Overview: click-drag a range directly on the chart (not just the existing TimeRangePicker control) to scope every KpiTile/DataTable below it — reuse the existing TimeRangePicker plumbing but add a draggable brush layer on BurnDownChart.
- Make sure every DataTable row on Overview/Cases-list/Dashboards deep-links straight into CaseDetail (Sentinel's 'grid as launchpad' pattern) — audit this is true everywhere, including custom-dashboard widgets (soc/dashboard/*).
- Keep dashboard auto-refresh OFF by default with an explicit user-set interval control (5m/15m/1h/1d) next to the existing 60s visibility-aware refresh, and make sure it resets to off whenever a dashboard view is closed/reopened — mirrors Sentinel's workbook auto-refresh discipline and keeps our real-time/SSE cost bounded.
- Noise Reduction funnel: model stages directly off decide()'s own vocabulary — Alerts ingested → Correlated into cases (signature match) → Auto-closed FALSE_POSITIVE (policy) → Escalated NEEDS_HUMAN → Confirmed TRUE_POSITIVE/Resolved. At each stage show count + %-of-previous-stage retained AND a small P50/P90 time-in-stage pair underneath (Sentinel's percentile idea applied per-funnel-stage) — use the dataviz skill to pick the actual chart primitive (funnel/Sankey), but keep volumetric encoding (counts) and time encoding (percentiles) as two separate visual channels, never overlaid on one shape.
- Give the funnel's auto-closed stage a distinct 'deterministic' badge/icon (shield/lock) tied to our existing severity/status/verdict semantic-token system, and give any stage influenced by LLM judgment (routing, investigator verdict) a distinct 'AI-assisted' badge — this directly operationalizes non-negotiable #3 (decide() is deterministic, never raw LLM output) as a literal, hoverable UI signal on the funnel itself, which Sentinel has no equivalent of and is a genuine differentiator to lean into.
- Add a hover tooltip on each funnel stage boundary showing the TOP closing/classification reasons for what dropped out at that stage (reuse Sentinel's 'Similarity reason' hover-popover pattern) so an analyst can see WHY without leaving the dashboard.
- Case story view: restructure CaseDetail's default/primary tab (not just the secondary 'Trace' tab) into Sentinel's 3-zone spatial split — LEFT persistent rail: severity/status/assignee controls + entity list + links into raw evidence (existing evidence/log drawer); CENTER: a chronological fact timeline (extend TraceTimeline) where every node is a system fact (ingested → correlated → risk-scored → LLM verdict emitted → decide() outcome), using the icon convention (shield=deterministic engine step, sparkle=LLM output) consistently on the PRIMARY story tab, not hidden in a separate Trace tab most analysts won't open; RIGHT rail: a persistent (not tab-gated) analytical panel merging threat-context/enrichment/RAG-rationale (already exposed via GET /api/cases/{id}/rationale) so facts and AI-derived assessment are always visible side-by-side rather than one click away.
- Add a collapsed-by-default 'AI Summary' card pinned at the top of the case story (Security Copilot pattern): auto-generated narrative with 'Show more', visually tinted using the existing verdict semantic token, and — critically — every entity/IOC/MITRE-technique named in the summary text rendered as a clickable chip that jumps to that fact in the left/center evidence spine. This both matches best-in-class UX and gives a visible, auditable trace back to the fenced UNTRUSTED evidence the LLM was given (reinforcing non-negotiable #9 in the UI, not just the prompt layer).
- Restyle HITL Proposals / threshold_automation recommendations / playbook suggestions as Sentinel-style 'guided response' cards: recommended action + target entity + WHY (rationale/evidence pointer) + a clear past-tense 'already applied automatically' state vs. an actionable pending-approval state, plus a thumbs-up/down affordance wired into the existing Case.feedback loop, and permission-gated buttons rendered grayed-out (not hidden) per our RBAC model.
- Keep any node-link 'investigation graph' view for a case strictly opt-in (an 'Investigate' button off the case story), never the default landing — per Microsoft's own admission the graph costs extra context-switching. If/when built, use solid edges for directly-OCSF-evidenced relationships vs dotted edges for analyst/enrichment-expanded ones, and expand incrementally on click (one hop at a time) rather than rendering the full entity graph up front.
- Render MITRE technique badges on the case header as plain, unstyled fact chips (consistent with existing MitreHeatmap treatment) rather than AI-flagged — the fact/derived visual distinction should track provenance (our deterministic engine/mitre.py mapping vs. actual LLM inference), not merely 'a model touched this at some point.'
- Roll out the CaseDetail story-view restructuring behind a user preference / feature-flag with an 'old view' fallback during the transition, matching Microsoft's own legacy/new-incident-experience toggle for a page this central to daily analyst workflow.

**Pitfalls to avoid:**
- Reporting only mean/average latency (MTTA/MTTR) — a single average hides P90/P99 outliers that are exactly the SLA breaches a SOC manager needs to see; always pair mean with percentiles.
- Blending AI-generated narrative and hard facts in one undifferentiated block of prose with no clickable citations — an analyst can't audit an unlinked AI claim; every entity/IOC the AI narrative names must link back to its fenced evidence.
- Making the node-link investigation graph the default/primary case view — Microsoft's own docs concede it costs heavy context-switching; it should be an opt-in secondary surface, not the landing tab.
- Building one universal dashboard for every persona instead of distinct default layouts per role (manager vs. analyst vs. asset owner) — Microsoft explicitly warns against this.
- Rendering a fully-expanded entity/relationship graph up front instead of incremental click-to-expand traversal — clutters the view and defeats the 'blast radius on demand' pattern Sentinel's 2026 graph GA moved to.
- Overloading a single funnel/chart shape with two different encodings (volume AND time-in-stage) — keep count-based funnel geometry and percentile/time metrics as separate adjacent visual elements.
- Defaulting dashboards/widgets to always-on auto-refresh regardless of visibility — costly and unnecessary; make refresh opt-in with a user-chosen interval, consistent with Sentinel workbooks and our own visibility-aware refresh discipline.
- Treating 'AI-derived' styling as a proxy for 'a model was involved anywhere' rather than for 'this fact is not deterministically guaranteed' — e.g., MITRE-technique tagging and decide()'s auto-close outcome are deterministic in our system and should read as plain fact, not be sparkle-badged just because an LLM verdict fed into the pipeline upstream.
- Hard-cutting-over a redesign of the primary case/incident page without a fallback toggle — this is the page analysts live in all day; ship it behind a preference flag first.

**Sources:**
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/sentinel/investigate-incidents
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/sentinel/entity-pages
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/sentinel/sentinel-security-copilot-incident-summary
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/defender-xdr/security-copilot-m365d-guided-response
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/sentinel/manage-soc-with-incident-metrics
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/sentinel/monitor-your-data
- https://www.itprofessor.cloud/sentinel-workbooks-three-essential/
- https://techcommunity.microsoft.com/blog/microsoft-security-blog/introducing-microsoft-sentinel-graph-public-preview/4456368
- https://techcommunity.microsoft.com/blog/microsoft-security-blog/announcing-public-preview-of-custom-graphs-in-microsoft-sentinel/4507410
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/sentinel/datalake/sentinel-graph-overview
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/sentinel/automate-incident-handling-with-automation-rules
- https://techcommunity.microsoft.com/blog/microsoftsentinelblog/the-new-incident-experience-is-here/3717042

## Google Chronicle / Google SecOps — UX patterns for alert triage, case view, and dashboards (informing Round-7 TLSOC UI overhaul)


**Key findings:**
- Case Overview is a WIDGET GRID, not a wall of text: Google SecOps' Case Overview tab is composed of discrete, purpose-built widgets — Gemini Summary, Entities Graph, Entity Highlights, Latest Case Wall Activity, Recommendations (similar cases/analysts), Statistics (distribution charts), an HTML widget (renders playbook output as formatted HTML, never raw JSON), and Quick Actions (one-click buttons for common workflows). Each widget has ONE job; nothing mixes raw evidence with narrative in the same box. (docs.cloud.google.com/chronicle/docs/soar/investigate/working-with-cases/cases-2.0-overview; security.googlecloudcommunity.com secops-enterprise-25 Step 4.2 walkthrough)
- The AI case summary is a fixed 3-part narrative, not free text: Gemini's Case Summary widget always renders exactly three labeled sections — 'What Actually Happened?' (a narrative synthesis), 'Case Activity' (what's been done so far), and 'Next and Pending Actions' (recommended remediation/next steps). This fixed schema is what makes an AI summary scannable instead of a wall of prose. (docs.cloud.google.com/.../using-the-gemini-case-summary-widget; medium.com/@thatsiemguy Q1'26 Gemini updates; community 2026-06 release notes)
- The Triage/Investigation Agent (TIN) renders its reasoning as a TIMELINE OF CARDS, each card = one discrete analysis step, each with a disposition-relevant source link back to the underlying evidence (e.g. a GTI lookup result or the exact search query executed) — so an analyst can click through from a claim straight to the fact that backs it. Feedback (thumbs up/down) is anchored to the SEVERITY VERDICT specifically, not a generic 'rate this case' control. (docs.cloud.google.com/chronicle/docs/secops/triage-investigation-agent; docs.cloud.google.com/chronicle/docs/investigation/investigate-alert)
- Facts vs. assessment are separated by PLACEMENT and WIDGET TYPE, not just color: raw alerts + curated detections live in a dedicated 'Detections' tab; raw event fields (Name/Type/Source/Port/Outcome/Time, grouped into Highlighted/Default/System/Threat/Event/Time field categories) live in an 'Events' sub-tab; the entity relationship graph is its own 'Graph' sub-tab; and only the Gemini Summary widget carries AI-authored narrative. Case Wall is a strictly chronological, append-only log of actions/status-changes/tasks/comments/playbook runs — i.e. the audit trail — kept visually and structurally distinct from the AI assessment.
- Risk is a normalized 1–1000 score with named bands surfaced everywhere, not a bespoke gauge per screen: roughly Low 1–300 ('monitor'), Medium 310–790 ('warrants attention'), Critical 800+ ('immediate investigation'); default base risk score is 40 for alerts / 15 for detections before entity aggregation. The entity risk timeline is read with explicit pattern language — 'plateau vs. spike' — to distinguish a sustained compromise from a one-off anomaly, and related alerts are 'composited' (grouped) on that timeline specifically to reduce visual clutter. (docs.cloud.google.com/chronicle/docs/detection/risk-analytics-overview + risk-analytics-dashboard; community 'Adoption Guide: Risk Analytics' 6589)
- Alert-to-case noise reduction is a named, layered pipeline the product is explicit about: (1) alert overflow/rate-limiting for burst duplicates (brute-force/DDoS floods), (2) alert suppression (rule-based filter of duplicative/low-value alerts), (3) throttling (suppress repeats of the same detection within a time window), (4) composite detections (correlation rules like 'only alert if X happens 5x for the same user in 1h'), then (5) mutual-entity + time-proximity grouping into a single 'threat-centric' case. Google frames the entire chain as 'signal over noise' and ties it to measurable analyst-fatigue reduction. (docs.cloud.google.com/.../define-alert-overflow-admin; .../alert-suppression; .../composite-detections; community 'Adoption Guide: Intelligent Alert Suppression')
- Case list/queue uses low-chrome, hover-reveal affordances: a persistent color swatch/bar keyed to case priority (clickable to change), list vs. side-by-side view toggle, and a hover-triggered case preview — the same 'hidden until hover' philosophy is used on native-dashboard tiles (drag handle, resize corner, and edit/duplicate/delete controls all invisible until the tile/row is hovered), keeping the default view visually calm. (docs.cloud.google.com/.../what-actions-can-you-take-on-a-case; community 'Building Our First Dashboard Tile' 4049)
- Curated, templated dashboards lower time-to-value: Google ships ready-made dashboards (Context Aware Detections–Risk, Data Ingestion & Health, IOC Matches, Main) plus a separate MITRE ATT&CK Enterprise Visualization Dashboard (a coverage heatmap over the whole matrix), so analysts start from a populated screen, not a blank canvas. SOC posture metrics are standardized: time-to-assign and MTTR are both shown in dd:hh:mm:ss, plus average handling time broken out by role/stage. (community 'Intro to Native Dashboards' 3934; docs.cloud.google.com/chronicle/docs/detection/mitre-dashboard; docs.cloud.google.com/chronicle/docs/reports/curated-dashboards/*)
- A widely-reported UX weakness of Gemini-in-SecOps (useful as a contrast, not a pattern to copy): the AI side panel is a binary 20%/80% width toggle with no continuous resize (practitioners resort to browser split-view as a workaround), the assistant has no persistent memory of the open case unless content is explicitly pasted into it, and — most notably — 'SecOps Gemini is currently a black box': it does NOT expose a chain-of-thought/reasoning trace, which the author explicitly calls out as a missed trust-building opportunity. (medium.com/@thatsiemguy 'Google SecOps Gemini Updates Q1 26')
- Curated detections carry MITRE Tactic/Technique columns and a Precise-vs-Broad rule variant with a per-rule alerting toggle, directly in the detections list — so ATT&CK coverage and alert-noise tuning are visible at the rule level, not just the dashboard level. (docs.cloud.google.com/chronicle/docs/detection/curated-detections + docs.cloud.google.com/chronicle/docs/detection/mitre-dashboard)

**Concrete recommendations for us:**
- Rebuild the CaseDetail 'Why'/story surface around Gemini's fixed 3-section schema: a `CaseStorySummary` card with exactly three labeled, always-present sections — 'What Happened' (deterministic facts: cluster signature, entity list, OCSF summary — pull straight from the case's evidence, no LLM prose here), 'Assessment' (the LLM verdict/confidence/reasoning excerpt — visually tagged with an 'AI' chip, reusing the existing muted/secondary surface token, not a new color axis), and 'Next Actions' (the deterministic decide() outcome + any pending Proposal/HITL items). This maps directly onto data you already compute (`GET /api/cases/{id}/rationale`, `engine/case_manager.decide()`) — it's a re-layout of an existing `webui/src/soc/pages/CaseDetail.tsx` + rationale consumer, not a new backend endpoint.
- Enforce a strict fact/assessment placement rule in CaseDetail, modeled on Google's tab separation: keep raw OCSF/evidence rendering (monospace, JetBrains Mono, from `unmapped`/`raw_data`, already UNTRUSTED-fenced per non-negotiable #9) in its own 'Evidence' section distinct from 'Assessment' — never let a single card mix a fenced log excerpt and free-form AI prose. Use `webui/src/soc/components/badges.tsx` verdict/status/severity tokens for facts and a distinct low-saturation 'ai-generated' treatment (e.g. a dashed border or an `AiChip` label, not a new hue) for anything LLM-authored, so a glance tells you which is which.
- Build the 'Noise Reduction' funnel infographic as a literal 5-stage horizontal funnel matching your real pipeline stages and Google's named noise-reduction layers: Raw Events → Correlated Clusters (`engine/correlation.py`) → Risk-Scored Candidates (`engine/risk.py` + `cost_gate.py`) → LLM-Investigated → Deterministic Decision (auto-closed FP / escalated TP / NEEDS_HUMAN). Annotate each stage with a live count + % drop from the previous stage (reuse `KpiTile`/`StatCard`), the same 'signal over noise' story Google tells with alert-overflow → suppression → throttling → composite-detection → threat-centric-case. Use the dataviz skill's funnel/stage-chart guidance for the visual.
- Extend `RiskGauge` to show named bands (not just a needle): label the scale explicitly (e.g. Low / Medium / Critical thresholds drawn from your own `engine/priority.py` derivation), mirroring Chronicle's 1–1000 / Low 1–300 / Medium 310–790 / Critical 800+ scheme — this teaches analysts the scale at a glance instead of requiring them to memorize an unlabeled gauge. Add a small per-signature risk sparkline in CaseDetail sourced from `engine/baseline.py`'s EWMA/EWMV history, and caption spikes vs. sustained elevation in text ('spike' vs 'sustained') the way Chronicle's entity-risk timeline does.
- Add a `QuickActionsCard` to CaseDetail (Google's Case-Overview 'Quick Actions' widget pattern): one-click buttons for Acknowledge, Escalate, Add to Campaign (surfacing `engine/campaigns.py` membership), Propose Rule Tuning (launching a `threshold_tuner` HITL Proposal directly, not requiring a detour to Settings→Rules), and the existing unified Close-with-disposition dialog — keep the 'one primary CTA' rule from Round 4 but make these secondary actions genuinely one-click from the case itself.
- Anchor case feedback to the specific claim being graded, not the whole case: put thumbs up/down (or a small agree/disagree control) directly next to the verdict chip and separately next to the risk/priority chips in the case header, wired to the existing `Case.feedback` model — mirror TIN's pattern of feedback-on-the-verdict-specifically so `POST /api/cases/{id}/feedback` captures WHICH claim was wrong, useful input for your own future auto-tuning loop.
- Ship 3–4 curated starter templates for the Round-5 custom-dashboards feature (`stores/dashboards.py`, `pages/Dashboards.tsx`) instead of only a blank canvas: 'SOC Command Center' (case/severity/verdict mix + KPIs), 'Ingestion & Source Health' (poller lag, per-source browse status), 'MITRE Coverage' (embed the existing `MitreHeatmap`), and 'Cost & Model Usage' (LLM ledger). This directly follows Google's curated-dashboard time-to-value pattern (Context Aware Detections–Risk / Ingestion & Health / IOC Matches / Main) and gives every role a populated default rather than an empty grid on first login.
- Apply the 'hidden until hover' chrome-reduction pattern to the Cases DataTable and to dashboard-tile editing: row actions (assign/tag/escalate) and priority color-swatch should be visible on hover/focus, not permanently rendered as extra columns — reduces visual noise on a dense list, matching both Chronicle's case list and its dashboard-tile edit affordances (drag handle, resize corner, edit/duplicate/delete only appear on hover).
- Surface campaign membership as a collapsible group header in the case list (not just a chip inside CaseDetail): 'N related cases in Campaign X' collapsing multiple rows into one expandable group, directly analogous to Chronicle's 'composite' grouping of related alerts on the entity risk timeline to cut visual clutter — you already compute campaign membership (`engine/campaigns.py`); this is a list-level rendering change.
- Add hover tooltips / click-through on `MitreHeatmap` cells listing the contributing case IDs or detection rules for that technique, matching Chronicle's Curated-Detections list which shows per-rule MITRE Tactic/Technique columns and its separate ATT&CK coverage heatmap — turns the heatmap from a static coverage view into a navigable one.
- Treat the case-side AI chat/reasoning panel as a competitive differentiator, not a feature to trim: unlike Gemini-in-SecOps (binary 20/80 width, no exposed reasoning, no case memory unless pasted), your `TraceTimeline` already exposes the deterministic `decide()` step and a typed ReAct trace — keep pushing this further by making each trace card a clickable link to the exact tool call (es_query/enrich/rag_retrieve) params + result that produced it, and make the panel continuously resizable (drag divider, remembered per-user via `UserPrefsStore`) instead of a fixed split, explicitly avoiding the documented Gemini-SecOps pain points.

**Pitfalls to avoid:**
- Don't give the AI side-panel/chat only a binary width toggle (e.g. 20%/80%) — Google SecOps practitioners explicitly complain about this and resort to browser split-view workarounds; use a continuously resizable, remembered-width panel instead.
- Don't leave AI reasoning as a black box — Google's own Gemini-in-SecOps is criticized for exactly this; since your product already surfaces the deterministic `decide()` step and a ReAct trace as a trust feature, protect and extend that rather than simplifying it away.
- Don't render raw JSON/log payloads directly in a case UI — Google explicitly avoids this via dedicated HTML-rendering widgets; always transform evidence into structured tables/fields, reserving raw/fenced blocks for an explicitly-labeled 'raw evidence' pane only.
- Don't mix AI-authored narrative and deterministic facts inside the same widget/card — Google keeps Entities Graph (facts), Case Wall (chronological human/system activity), Detections tab (raw alerts), and Gemini Summary (AI narrative) as structurally separate surfaces; interleaving them erodes the fact/assessment trust boundary this whole overhaul is meant to establish.
- Don't ship a custom-dashboard builder with only a blank canvas — Google's curated starter dashboards are a deliberate time-to-value device; a blank grid on first login reads as unfinished.
- Don't use a generic whole-case thumbs-up/down — anchor feedback to the specific claim (verdict vs. risk vs. priority) being graded, the way TIN's feedback is scoped specifically to the severity verdict, or you lose the signal you'd need for any future tuning loop.
- Don't rely on a text badge alone for case priority in a dense list — a persistent color swatch/left-edge stripe (Google's clickable priority color swatch) scans far faster than a column of text badges.

**Sources:**

## Elastic Security (SIEM) — overview dashboard, alert/case UX, and the Timeline investigation view: patterns to borrow for TLSOC's Round-7 UI overhaul


**Key findings:**
- Detection & Response dashboard is a 6-panel 'command center' grammar, not generic BI: (1) Alerts panel = count-in-range × status × severity, (2) Cases panel = count-in-range × status, (3) 'Open Alerts by Rule' = ranked top-4 leaderboard (rule, severity, count) not a chart, (4) 'Recently Created Cases' = top-4 mini-list, (5) 'Hosts by Alert Severity' = ranked bar list up to 100 hosts, (6) 'Users by Alert Severity' = same for users. Every number is clickable and drills straight into a pre-filtered Alerts/Cases page — the dashboard is an entry funnel into the queues, not an end state. (elastic.co/docs/solutions/security/dashboards/detection-response-dashboard)
- Overview dashboard pairs a 'Live feed' rail (recently created cases + favorited Timelines + product news) next to time-based histograms with a 'Stack by' selector (e.g. stack by rule.name) so the SAME chart re-slices in place without a new query/panel, plus a dedicated Threat-Intelligence tile (# indicators ingested, # sources enabled, per-source breakdown). (elastic.co/guide/en/security/current/overview-dashboard.html)
- Every chart/table in Elastic Security exposes an 'Inspect' affordance revealing the exact underlying query (ES DSL / ES|QL) that produced it, plus an options menu to pin the panel into a case or open it in Lens for ad-hoc rework. This is a deliberate trust/provenance pattern surfaced everywhere, not a hidden debug feature. (elastic.co/docs/solutions/security/get-started/elastic-security-ui)
- The alert-details flyout is a strict 3-panel architecture (right = overview, left = expand-in-place detail, preview = transient overlay) with an explicit 'About' section ladder: Rule description (static) → Alert reason (a DETERMINISTICALLY composed plain-English sentence built straight from the source event's fields, not LLM output, always available at zero cost) → Investigation guide (a per-rule-type static playbook) → Highlighted fields. AI only enters later, via an explicit 'Chat with AI Assistant' action, never blended into the About section. (elastic.co/docs/solutions/security/detect-and-alert/view-detection-alert-details)
- The flyout's 'Insights' rail is 4 clearly-named, separately-sourced cards: Entities (host/user identity + optional risk score), Threat Intelligence (matched IOC feed hits), Correlations (suppressed-alert count, related source events, related cases, shared session/process ancestry), Prevalence (is this field value seen on <10% of hosts = uncommon, or common). Each card names its data source in its header — nothing is unattributed.
- Timeline (the investigation workspace) = KQL query bar + a spatial query builder (horizontally-adjacent filters = AND, vertically-stacked = OR) + a customizable-column event table + an EQL-powered Correlation tab that visually shades matched event sequences (red/blue groups) ordered oldest→newest + toggleable 'event renderers' (pre-built mini-visualizations per event type, e.g. network-flow renderer) + drag-and-drop fields, pinning, and per-event notes, all attachable to a Case to preserve the investigative narrative. (elastic.co/guide/en/security/current/timelines-ui.html)
- Every data field throughout the UI (table cells, flyout fields) exposes hover actions: Filter In / Filter Out, Add to Timeline, Show top-N values, Copy to clipboard — one consistent interaction vocabulary reused everywhere instead of bespoke per-page actions.
- Attack Discovery (LLM correlates many raw alerts into one narrative) presents each 'discovery' as: title + prose summary + associated-alert count + MITRE ATT&CK mapping + implicated entities + an expandable 'Alerts preview' of the underlying raw alerts. BUT per Elastic's own docs the AI narrative and the underlying alert facts are visually integrated/blended in this view, not clearly boundaried — a documented gap, not a strength, and useful as a 'what not to copy' anti-example. (elastic.co/docs/solutions/security/ai/attack-discovery)
- Elastic's own 'agentic SOC' reference architecture (Security Labs, 2025-2026) is the single most directly-relevant source: a 4-stage pipeline — (1) deterministic ES|QL pre-filter closes known-FP patterns at ZERO token cost ('any check that can be resolved by a query should be a query, not an LLM call'); (2) a narrow Initial-Triage agent emits ONE verdict (true/false positive) + assessment (malicious/suspicious/unknown/benign) + confidence (high/low), auto-closing only high-confidence FPs; (3) specialized fact-only agents (macOS/Windows forensics, CloudTrail, Okta, Azure, GCP...) each add a case comment that is explicitly forbidden from stating a verdict — 'no verdict, assessment, or judgment', purely 'what happened'; (4) a Final-Review synthesis agent, constrained to ONLY the facts already in the case (preventing hallucination/independent investigation), writes the analyst-facing case summary: verdict header, relationship analytics, recommended actions, event timeline. Elastic's stated design rationale: 'Keeping facts and verdict in separate agents prevents the interpretation in one domain's findings from biasing the final call.' This is architecturally identical to TLSOC's cost_gate → router → investigator → case_manager.decide() pipeline and directly validates a fact/verdict-separated case narrative. (elastic.co/security-labs/alert-triage-agentic-soc-elastic-workflows)
- Elastic's Cases model: alerts/files/observables/visualizations all attach to one case; an Observables tab holds up to 50 typed indicators (IP, hash, URL...) used to spot correlated events across cases; Timelines attach to preserve investigative narrative; a dedicated Alerts tab lists attached alerts oldest→newest. (elastic.co/docs/solutions/security/investigate/security-cases)
- Elastic's own published post-mortem on generative-AI output presentation is candid about a gap: heavy backend rigor (LLM-as-judge evaluation, ≥85% accuracy gating, auto-regeneration on failed rubric checks) but the blog itself notes 'minimal detail about frontend transparency indicators... no discussion of confidence scores, uncertainty warnings, or how analysts visually distinguish AI-generated narrative from raw alert data within the interface.' Elastic invests in AI QUALITY but under-invests in AI-vs-fact UI legibility — exactly the gap our 'clean case story that separates SIEM-asserted facts from AI-derived assessment' goal should close. (elastic.co/blog/elastic-security-generative-ai-features)

**Concrete recommendations for us:**
- Command Center dashboard — copy the Detection & Response 6-panel grammar directly onto our Overview page: row 1 = KpiTile strip for {Open cases by status, Alerts/cases in range by severity}; row 2 = two ranked-leaderboard widgets (NEW component: `RankedList` — rule/signature name + severity dot + count, top-5, click-through to a pre-filtered Cases table) for 'Top signatures by open-case volume' and 'Top entities (host/user) by severity' sourced from engine/metrics.py + engine/priority.py; row 3 = 'Recently created cases' mini-list (reuse CaseHoverCard rows) + our existing RiskGauge/BurnDownChart trend. Every tile/row must be clickable straight into Cases with the filter pre-applied (status, severity, signature, entity) — mirror Elastic's 'dashboard as funnel entry point, not a dead end' principle.
- Add an 'Inspect' affordance (a small icon button, top-right of every chart/KpiTile/RankedList row, shadcn `Popover` or `HoverCard`) that shows the literal query/aggregation that produced the number — for us that's the ES/OpenSearch query from tools/es_query.py or the metrics.py aggregation pipeline. This is cheap to build (we already log tool calls into the audit trail per non-negotiable #2) and directly reinforces analyst trust the way Elastic's ubiquitous Inspect button does.
- Give the trend chart(s) a 'Stack by' control (severity | verdict | source | signature) reusing the existing charts.tsx component rather than shipping N separate chart variants — one chart, a segmented control (we already have `SegmentedControl` primitive from Round 5) to re-slice in place, exactly like Elastic's histogram stack-by.
- Noise Reduction funnel infographic — render it as a literal 4-to-5 stage decreasing-width funnel/Sankey (dataviz skill: use a sequential palette, label absolute counts AND %, not just %) that mirrors Elastic's own agentic-SOC stage breakdown 1:1 onto our real pipeline stages: Stage 1 'Ingested alerts' → Stage 2 'Deterministic pre-filter (cost_gate + correlation/signature match)' with an annotated pull-quote-style caption ('resolved by a query, not an LLM call — $0') and the count/percentage dropped for free → Stage 3 'Router triage (cheap LLM verdict)' with $ cost for this stage from the usage/cost ledger → Stage 4 'Investigator (strong LLM, ReAct)' count + $ cost → Stage 5 'Case decide()' output split into auto-closed FP / auto-escalated TP / NEEDS_HUMAN. Label the deterministic stages with a distinct (non-LLM) icon/color the whole product already uses for 'deterministic' vs 'LLM' steps in TraceTimeline, so the funnel and the per-case trace timeline share one visual vocabulary for 'this step cost money' vs 'this step was free/deterministic'.
- Source the funnel's per-stage $ and count numbers live from llm/gateway.py's usage ledger and engine/cost_gate.py rather than hand-waving — this is a real differentiator vs Elastic's marketing framing ('30 min → under 3 min') because we can show ACTUAL dollars-saved-by-determinism per period, tying directly into our existing Cost page.
- Case story view — restructure CaseDetail's narrative stream (CaseThread) into Elastic's fact/verdict-separated authoring model: render each entry as one of exactly 3 visually distinct card types — (a) SIEM FACT card (grey/neutral border, monospace field=value chips or a compact table, sourced from raw OCSF/enrichment/tool-call results, explicitly labeled with its data source e.g. 'Enrichment: VirusTotal' / 'ES|QL query result') — never contains a verdict word; (b) AI ASSESSMENT card (accent-colored border matching our verdict/status token axis, carries a verdict pill + confidence badge + the reasoning prose, explicitly labeled 'AI — investigator persona: X') ; (c) DETERMINISTIC DECISION card (a third, distinct style — this is decide() itself, already partially done via TraceTimeline's 'deterministic step' — make it visually the most prominent/pinned card, since it's the one thing that's authoritative per non-negotiable #3). This 3-way split is literally what Elastic's own agentic-SOC blueprint does (specialized agents = fact cards with 'no verdict' by design; Initial Triage + Final Review = assessment cards; and unlike Elastic, we can go further by giving the CaseManager decide() output its own pinned, unmistakable card style, since Elastic doesn't have an equivalent non-LLM authority step to highlight).
- Add a static, deterministically-composed 'reason' line at the top of CaseDetail (above any AI content, zero LLM cost) that assembles directly from OCSF fields — e.g. 'Alert triggered: {signature} on {host} by {user}, {n} correlated events in {window}' — exactly matching Elastic's alert-reason pattern, so every case has an always-available, zero-cost, human-readable one-liner even before/without any LLM investigation ran (useful when cost_gate defers/denies a case to NEEDS_HUMAN).
- Build a 4-card 'Insights' rail on CaseDetail mirroring Elastic's Entities/Threat-Intel/Correlations/Prevalence layout, backed by our OWN existing engines so it's not vaporware: Entities → engine/priority.py + entity identity; Threat Intelligence → enrichment/aggregate.py fused indicator verdicts (already have 19 providers — just needs a compact card); Correlations → engine/campaigns.py (which cases share this campaign) + engine/correlation strategies; Prevalence → engine/baseline.py's z-score / percentile-of-normal output re-labeled in Elastic's plain-English framing ('seen on 2 of 340 hosts this period — uncommon'). Each card header must name its provenance exactly like Elastic does, never an unattributed number.
- Add the same 5 hover-actions Elastic puts on every field (Filter in/out, Add to timeline/case-evidence, Show top-N, Copy) as a small consistent action-menu on every evidence chip/table cell across DataTable and CaseDetail's fact cards — implement ONCE as a shared `FieldActions` component (small popover triggered by hover+click) rather than bespoke per-page context menus, and additionally add a TLSOC-specific 6th action Elastic doesn't have: 'Pivot to related cases sharing this entity' (wired to engine/campaigns.py / threat_context.py), since that's a capability we already have that Elastic's UI doesn't surface as a single click.
- For the Timeline-equivalent trace view (TraceTimeline), adopt Elastic's Correlation-tab visual grammar: color-shade groups of events/tool-calls that belong to the same correlation cluster or campaign, ordered oldest→newest, so an analyst can visually see 'these 4 tool calls + 2 enrichments belong to one reasoning chain' the way Elastic shades matched EQL sequences red/blue.
- Give the Command Center's KPI row a fixed-column CSS grid (not flex-wrap) with a min-width sized for the max expected digit count (Elastic shipped a bug in 8.19 where KPI panels overflowed their containers and left ragged whitespace on the Alerts/Attacks pages) — bake the fix in from day one rather than discovering it later.
- Single-source severity/risk/priority computation: Elastic has an open GitHub issue (elastic/kibana#122011) where severity/risk-score values differ between the alerts table and the alert-details flyout because they're computed/rendered in two places. For TLSOC, make sure the Command Center KPI tiles, the case list row, and CaseDetail's chips all read the SAME computed field (engine/priority.py's derived severity/impact/urgency/priority, already 'advisory, never feeds decide()') rather than each view recomputing or reading a stale duplicate — add a regression test asserting list-view and detail-view render byte-identical severity/priority for the same case_id.

**Pitfalls to avoid:**
- Do not blend AI-generated narrative directly into the same visual container as raw alert facts the way Elastic's own Attack Discovery view does — Elastic's docs describe title+summary+entities+alert-preview all inside one expandable card with no strong visual boundary between 'the LLM said' and 'the raw alerts show'; this is a documented weak spot, not a pattern to emulate.
- Do not ship AI assessment cards with prose-only reasoning and no explicit confidence/verdict affordance separate from the narrative text — Elastic's own retrospective blog admits their frontend has 'minimal... confidence scores, uncertainty warnings, or how analysts visually distinguish AI-generated narrative from raw alert data' despite heavy backend eval rigor; don't inherit that gap. Always render verdict + confidence as a structured badge/pill distinct from the free-text reasoning paragraph.
- Do not let specialized/fact-gathering steps sneak a verdict word into their output — Elastic's agentic SOC blueprint explicitly forbids this ('no verdict, assessment, or judgment' in specialized-agent comments) specifically to prevent one domain's framing from biasing the final synthesis; if our investigator's intermediate tool-call summaries start editorializing ('this looks malicious'), that contaminates the fact/verdict separation the whole story-view design depends on.
- Do not duplicate severity/risk/priority computation across list view, detail view, and dashboard KPI — Elastic shipped exactly this bug (kibana#122011: alerts table vs alert-details flyout showing different severity/risk for the same alert); single-source it.
- Do not build dashboard KPI tiles with flex-wrap/auto-width that can overflow on 3-digit counts — Elastic shipped this exact whitespace/overflow bug on the Alerts/Attacks pages in 8.19 and had to patch it; use a fixed grid sized for worst-case digit counts from the start.
- Do not treat the 'Inspect the query' transparency affordance as optional polish — it's present on essentially every Elastic visualization and is core to analyst trust in a system that auto-closes cases; if we skip it, our Command Center reads as more of a black box than the incumbent SIEM despite our stronger deterministic/audited backend (non-negotiable #2).
- Do not cap observables/evidence-entity attachments artificially low the way Elastic's Cases model does (hard cap of 50 observables/case) — since our own state backend has no such constraint, don't copy that limitation; if we DO want a cap for UI legibility (e.g. a collapsed 'show more'), make it a display affordance, not a hard data limit.
- Do not make the funnel infographic purely percentage-based with no absolute counts or dollar costs — Elastic's marketing framing ('30 minutes to under 3 minutes') is persuasive precisely because it's concrete; a percentage-only funnel reads as abstract. Always show n and $ per stage, sourced live from the cost ledger, not hardcoded example numbers.

**Sources:**
- https://www.elastic.co/docs/solutions/security/get-started/elastic-security-ui
- https://www.elastic.co/docs/solutions/security/dashboards
- https://www.elastic.co/docs/solutions/security/dashboards/detection-response-dashboard
- https://www.elastic.co/guide/en/security/current/detection-response-dashboard.html
- https://www.elastic.co/guide/en/security/current/overview-dashboard.html
- https://www.elastic.co/docs/solutions/security/detect-and-alert/view-detection-alert-details
- https://www.elastic.co/guide/en/security/current/timelines-ui.html
- https://www.elastic.co/docs/solutions/security/ai/attack-discovery
- https://www.elastic.co/docs/solutions/security/ai/triage-alerts
- https://www.elastic.co/security-labs/alert-triage-agentic-soc-elastic-workflows
- https://www.elastic.co/security-labs/streamlining-the-security-analyst-experience
- https://www.elastic.co/blog/elastic-security-generative-ai-features
- https://www.elastic.co/docs/solutions/security/investigate/security-cases
- https://github.com/elastic/kibana/issues/122011
- https://www.elastic.co/guide/en/security/8.19/release-notes-header-8.19.0.html
