## CrowdStrike Falcon console — detections dashboard and incident/detection detail UX (density, color, hierarchy) for the Round-7 TLSOC UI overhaul


**Key findings:**
- CrowdStrike's own PUBLIC design-system source (github.com/CrowdStrike/tailwind-toucan-base, src/themes.json — the real Tailwind preset that ships the Falcon console) exposes the actual production color tokens for both light and dark theme. Severity is a 5-step semantic axis, fully separate from every other palette: DARK theme critical=#fa4147, high=#f77d40, medium=#ffcc00, low=#cef2ce (pale mint), informational=#9dc1fd (pale blue). LIGHT theme critical=#e9181f, high=#ff7e00, medium=#edb000, low=#649f64 (solid mid-green), informational=#5082d2 (solid blue).
- Falcon does NOT derive its dark/light severity colors from one hue via a lightness multiplier — each theme has independently hand-tuned values per severity step, tuned for contrast against that theme's own background. Most strikingly, 'low' flips from a near-white pale-mint tint in dark mode to a fully saturated mid-green in light mode (mint-on-black reads fine; mint-on-white would fail AA) — same hue family, deliberately different lightness/saturation per theme, not a mechanical invert.
- Falcon keeps its brand accent (brand=#d31912 dark / #d31c12 light, CrowdStrike red) as a CSS variable completely distinct from the 'critical' severity token (#fa4147/#e9181f) even though both are red. Brand chrome and severity meaning are never the same variable, so decorative brand-red usage (logo, primary buttons) can never be visually confused with a critical-severity signal.
- Falcon's dark-theme hierarchy is a stepped-lightness 'surface ladder', not shadows alone: basement #131217 (darkest, page bg) → ground-floor #27262c → surface-inner #17161a (recessed/inset fields) → surface-base #26252a → surface-md #2d2c31 → surface-lg #343337 → surface-xl #38373c → surface-2xl #3d3c40 (topmost, e.g. a popover nested inside a drawer). Each nested layer of chrome gets progressively LIGHTER as it 'floats' higher. In the LIGHT theme almost all of those surface tokens collapse to plain #ffffff (only surface-inner ≈ #ededed for recessed fields) — hierarchy in light mode is instead carried by a 3-step border system (border-faint #dedee3 hairline dividers → border-reg #c8c8d0 card outlines → border-bold #9c9caa emphasis/focus) plus shadow, not fill-color steps. This is a documented, source-verified split strategy: dark mode = elevate via fill lightness, light mode = elevate via border/shadow weight.
- Falcon color-codes its process/event TIMELINE and graph by TELEMETRY CATEGORY, kept completely separate from the severity palette: process-operations (blue #629cfb dark/#1055c6 light), network-operations (pink/magenta #fb5093/#c70f57), dns-requests (violet #8556fe/#4902fd), disc(disk)-operations (purple #db62f9/#b308dd), registry-operations (indigo #5d63fe/#0108c6). A real analyst walkthrough (Medium/@pasanglamatamang, an actual SOC-analyst account of triaging a detection) confirms this in practice: the detection detail view showed literal counters 'Network Operations (50), Disk Operations (58), DNS Requests (28)' as distinctly colored category chips, alongside a highlighted orange 'Related Intelligence' tab — i.e. category color is orthogonal to, and never overloaded with, severity/verdict color.
- The Falcon detection DATA MODEL itself (confirmed via CrowdStrike's public API docs, developer.crowdstrike.com and CrowdStrike/falconpy on GitHub) hard-separates SEVERITY from CONFIDENCE as two independent numeric fields on every detection: max_severity/max_severity_displayname (how bad IF the behavior is real — a property of the behavior/policy) vs max_confidence (the ML model's certainty that the classification is correct) — plus deterministic MITRE tactic/technique/objective fields and an adversary_id, all sitting alongside the raw asserted behaviors[] array (process, command line, file path, hashes, host). This is architecturally the same separation TLSOC already encodes in decide()'s (verdict, confidence, risk_score, policy) tuple — CrowdStrike's own schema validates keeping 'confidence' and 'severity/risk' as visually and structurally distinct fields rather than folding them into one number.
- CrowdStrike's Charlotte AI Detection Triage (2025 GA feature, crowdstrike.com/en-us/blog/agentic-ai-innovation-in-cybersecurity-charlotte-ai-detection-triage) is the closest real-world analog to TLSOC's own AI-triage product: it autonomously evaluates every new detection and surfaces exactly four fields per case — priority level, true/false-positive classification, a confidence score, and a recommended course of action — explicitly marketed as producing 'traceable and auditable insights' with human override of automation rules. This four-field shape (priority / classification / confidence / recommended action) is a validated, market-tested minimum viable 'AI verdict card' shape.
- A real SOC-analyst detection walkthrough (Medium/@pasanglamatamang) shows the IOA (Indicator of Attack) explanation rendered as ONE plain sentence directly in the side inspector panel next to the verdict metadata — e.g. 'A domain lookup matched a CrowdStrike Intelligence indicator' — with the triggering indicator value shown inline, not buried behind a separate 'details' click. The explanation sits beside hostname/user/tactic/technique/timestamp in the same compact panel.
- The Toucan type scale used for Falcon's console body chrome is deliberately compact/dense (from tailwind.config.js: xxs=9px, xs=12px, sm=14px, md=16px, lg=20px, xl=24px, 2xl=32px, 3xl=40px, 4xl=48px) — an enterprise data-console convention of running body/table text at 12-14px, reserving 16px+ only for headings/hero numbers. The config also explicitly DROPS Tailwind's sm/md responsive breakpoints ('to reduce file size ~700kb'), keeping only lg=1120px/2xl=1536px — an explicit acknowledgment that this is a desktop-first enterprise console, not a mobile-responsive product.
- Real-user reviews (G2, aggregated 2025-2026) consistently flag the Falcon console as feeling 'cluttered' and 'overwhelming for newer users,' requiring analysts to 'dig' to understand what a given alert actually means, with a real learning curve before someone becomes fluent in where things live — even though the same reviewers praise its power and dashboard customizability once mastered. A professional CSO.com review separately singled out Falcon's cartoon 'Rogue's Gallery' threat-actor avatars as an unwelcome, too-cutesy note in an otherwise serious console.
- CrowdStrike's dashboard widget model (Tech Hub 'Customized Dashboards' article) is role/persona-scoped rather than one-size-fits-all: SOC-analyst dashboards pull single-source Activity/Detections widgets (New Detections, Prevented Malware by Host, Detections by Tactic) with dynamic severity+time-range filters and donut/line-chart visualizations; IT-admin dashboards combine Hosts+Investigate categories instead. Widgets are resizable/movable, and severity filtering (e.g. 'show only Critical+High') is a first-class, always-visible dashboard control, not buried in settings.

**Concrete recommendations for us:**
- Define severity as ONE semantic axis with independently authored light/dark values (5 steps: critical/high/medium/low/informational), each as its own CSS custom property (e.g. --severity-critical, --severity-high, ...) — do NOT compute the dark-mode value by lightening/darkening the light-mode hex programmatically. Hand-check each of the 10 resulting swatches (5 steps × 2 themes) against WCAG AA on ITS OWN background, the way CrowdStrike's 'low' token goes from pale-mint-on-black to solid-green-on-white. This is exactly the pattern our existing severity/status/verdict axis in webui/src/styles/theme.css already targets (Round 5 G1) — use these real vendor numbers as a calibration reference, not to copy verbatim (TLSOC's palette is its own), but to sanity-check that our per-theme severity values were tuned independently rather than derived by a single dark-mode filter.
- Keep the brand/accent color (whatever TLSOC's primary chrome color is) as a CSS variable that is NEVER reused for the 'critical' severity token, even if they happen to be a similar hue. Audit webui/src/styles/theme.css and palette.ts for any place where --primary or an accent token is being read by a severity/verdict component — split them if found, so a future rebrand can't accidentally desaturate or repaint what 'critical' means.
- Implement a stepped-lightness 'surface ladder' for CaseDetail/dashboard panel nesting in DARK theme specifically (page background darkest → card → nested drawer → popover-in-drawer, each ~3-6% lighter), rather than relying only on box-shadow for elevation in dark mode. In LIGHT theme, do the opposite: collapse almost all surface fills to white and carry hierarchy via a 3-step border-weight system (hairline/regular/bold) + shadow — do not try to run the same 8-step fill ladder in light mode, it will look washed out. This directly informs PageContainer/Card/Sheet/Popover token choices in webui/src/ui/* and soc/components/*.
- In the CaseDetail 'story' / trace-timeline view, assign each OCSF event category (process, network, dns, file/registry, auth) its own STABLE categorical color chip that is orthogonal to the severity/verdict palette — mirroring Falcon's process-operations/network-operations/dns-requests/disc-operations/registry-operations color-coding. Pull these from the existing Okabe-Ito/viridis categorical set (per the dataviz skill) rather than inventing new hues, and reserve red/orange/yellow strictly for the severity/verdict axis so category chips can never be misread as a severity signal.
- Build the case/detection 'AI Assessment' card as a Charlotte-AI-shaped 4-field minimum: Priority (severity/risk-derived), Verdict/classification (true_positive/false_positive/needs_human — this already exists), Confidence (numeric %, kept visually and structurally separate from severity/risk, matching CrowdStrike's max_severity vs max_confidence split and TLSOC's own decide(verdict, confidence, risk_score, policy) signature), and Recommended action/next-step. Render this as ONE distinct visual zone, clearly separated (e.g. a bordered 'AI Assessment' card) from a second 'Detected Facts' zone (hostname, process tree, raw command line, file hash, IOA/rule name, MITRE tactic/technique) that is asserted/deterministic SIEM data — this is the literal facts-vs-assessment separation the Round-7 brief asks for, and CrowdStrike's own detection schema (severity/confidence/tactic/technique as structured fields vs behaviors[] as raw asserted telemetry) is a validated real-world precedent for exactly this split.
- Put the one-sentence 'why' explanation for the AI verdict INLINE, directly beside the verdict badge in the case header/side panel — not hidden behind a separate 'Why' tab click. TLSOC already has a rationale endpoint/tab (GET /api/cases/{id}/rationale) — surface its top-line reasoning sentence as an always-visible caption under/next to the verdict chip on CaseDetail and in the cases list hover-card, the way Falcon shows 'A domain lookup matched a CrowdStrike Intelligence indicator' inline next to the IOA metadata, reserving the full tab/drawer for the deeper trace.
- Tighten default body/table typography in DataTable and case-list rows to 12-14px (Tailwind text-xs/text-sm) with tight row height (32-36px), reserving text-base/lg+ only for page titles and KPI hero numbers — matching Falcon's compact 9/12/14/16px scale. Audit webui/src/soc/components/DataTable.tsx and CaseDetail row/list components for any default text-base body copy that could be stepped down for density.
- Build the 'Security Command Center' overview as persona/role-scoped widgets pulling from a SINGLE category at a time (e.g. a Detections-only widget row with its own severity+time-range filter, separate from a Hosts/Sources widget row) rather than one giant mixed-source panel — mirroring Falcon's single-source-per-widget dashboard model — and make the Critical+High severity filter an always-visible primary control on the dashboard, not a buried setting, since that's the single most-used filter in real SOC workflows per CrowdStrike's own dashboard guidance.
- For the 'Noise Reduction' funnel infographic specifically: no public CrowdStrike screenshot of a literal alerts→cases funnel widget was found in this research (their public messaging talks in prose about event/detection volume and about Charlotte AI saving '40+ hours/week', not a rendered funnel chart) — so treat the funnel infographic as an ORIGINAL TLSOC design opportunity, not a copy of an observed Falcon UI. Design it around the same 4 fields Charlotte AI surfaces per detection (priority / classification / confidence / recommended action) as the labels attached to the funnel's terminal stage, so the funnel visually terminates in the same vocabulary the case detail view uses — reuse dataviz-skill funnel/Sankey guidance for the chart mechanics themselves.
- Explicitly avoid mobile-first responsive over-engineering for the dense dashboard/case views — Falcon's own Tailwind config deliberately drops sm/md breakpoints for exactly this class of enterprise console. If TLSOC's webui already treats desktop as primary (per CLAUDE.md, it does), this is a validated precedent to keep breakpoint complexity minimal for SOC-analyst-only surfaces (dashboard, CaseDetail, DataTable) while keeping the login/wizard responsive.

**Pitfalls to avoid:**
- Do not compute per-theme severity colors from a single hue via a mechanical dark/light multiplier — CrowdStrike's own tokens prove each theme needs independently authored lightness/saturation per severity step (their 'low' token is a completely different color family-weight between themes, not just inverted lightness); a naive HSL-invert will produce AA failures on one theme even if the other passes.
- Do not let the brand/primary accent color double as the 'critical' severity color, even 'temporarily' or 'because they're both red' — this dilutes the meaning of critical severity everywhere the brand color also appears (buttons, active nav item, links), exactly the confusion CrowdStrike's separate brand/critical tokens are designed to prevent.
- Do not rely on box-shadow alone for panel hierarchy in dark mode — in a genuinely dark theme, shadows are nearly invisible against a near-black background; CrowdStrike's answer is a fill-lightness ladder instead. Conversely, do not try to reuse that same fill-ladder approach in light mode — on a white background, stepping fill color from white to slightly-off-white barely reads, so light mode needs border/shadow-driven hierarchy instead. Using one strategy for both themes is a common and visible mistake.
- Do not fold 'confidence' and 'severity/risk' into a single number or a single visual affordance — real-world detection schemas (CrowdStrike's max_severity vs max_confidence) and TLSOC's own decide() signature both treat them as orthogonal; collapsing them into one badge loses information an analyst needs (a low-confidence CRITICAL alert should look and be triaged very differently from a high-confidence CRITICAL one).
- Do not bury the one-line 'why was this flagged' explanation behind an extra click/tab — real analyst accounts of using Falcon show the explanation living right next to the verdict metadata; per G2/user reviews, the #1 complaint about Falcon is exactly that some things take 'digging' to understand, which is the anti-pattern to avoid.
- Avoid cartoonish/mascot-style illustration (CrowdStrike's 'Rogue's Gallery' threat-actor cartoons drew explicit ridicule in a professional CSO.com review) in a tool meant to convey serious incident severity — keep iconography restrained (line icons, muted illustration) rather than playful avatars, especially anywhere near a CRITICAL/escalated case.
- Do not treat category color-coding (event/operation type: process/network/dns/file/registry) and severity color-coding as the same palette or the same visual language (e.g. both rendered as similarly-shaped colored pill badges) — analysts need to be able to tell at a glance 'this chip means WHAT KIND of event' vs 'this chip means HOW BAD', and reusing shape+saturation across both purposes defeats that.
- Do not over-invest in responsive mobile breakpoints for the dense analyst-facing dashboard/case-detail surfaces at the expense of information density on desktop — this is a documented, deliberate tradeoff CrowdStrike's own build makes (dropping sm/md breakpoints entirely) because the audience is desktop SOC analysts, not phone users.

**Sources:**

- https://github.com/CrowdStrike/falcon-styles (Falcon light/dark CSS custom-properties stylesheet, archived but source-readable)
- https://github.com/CrowdStrike/ember-toucan-styles

- https://developer.crowdstrike.com/falcon-mcp/modules/detections/ (Detections module: falcon_get_detection_details / falcon_search_detections / falcon_update_detections)

## Palo Alto Cortex XSIAM / XSOAR — incident "war room" UX, dashboard design, and how automation/AI actions are surfaced to analysts


**Key findings:**
- "Eliminate the swivel chair" is the explicit design goal: XSIAM's marketing and product tour state "all the information you need to investigate and resolve this incident is provided in one place" — one incident view aggregates alert stitching, risk score, MITRE mapping, playbook status, and artifacts rather than forcing tab-hopping across tools.
- Alert→incident noise reduction is the headline metric, presented as concrete before/after stat cards, not just a chart. PA's own case studies (Cortex XSIAM Solution Brief, 2023) show: "~1,000 alerts/day → ~250/day (75% reduction), eliminating false positives and duplicates," "MTTR: 3 days → 16 minutes (270x faster)," and "10x improvement in incident closure rate, <10% → 100%." The narrative: "alert grouping + AI-driven incident scoring seamlessly connects low-confidence events into high-confidence incidents."
- The incident view splits automation status into three explicit, always-visible buckets rather than one status field: actions "automatically taken" (done), actions "requiring analyst review" (pending/HITL), and "recommended actions" the system suggests going forward — plus a separate "Executions" tab that is a full chronological log of everything that ran (playbooks, commands, results). Source: Cortex XSIAM Product Tour (paloaltonetworks.com/resources/infographics/xsiam-product-tour).
- Risk scoring is explicitly made explainable, not a black box. "SmartScore" (introduced XDR 3.4 / XSIAM 1.2) computes a numeric incident risk score from context-driven alert features, incident-level stats (alert count/severity/source mix), and Cortex Data Lake prevalence stats. "SmartScore Explainability" (XDR 3.6 / XSIAM 1.4+) layers SHAP (SHapley Additive exPlanations) on top specifically to show WHICH factors contributed to the score, in plain language that needs "no pre-existing knowledge in data science" — explicitly framed by PA as a trust-building feature ("Unlocking the Black Box" blog). This is the single most relevant precedent for separating SIEM facts from AI assessment: the score itself is treated as a derived, decomposable claim with a visible factor list, not an opaque verdict.
- A causality/root-cause graph view is a dedicated tab ("Causality View"/"Executions") distinct from the alert list: nodes represent processes, alerts, and actions; edges show the causal chain, so the analyst can see WHY disparate alerts were grouped into one incident, not just that they were.
- XSOAR's "War Room" is a single chronological ChatOps feed per incident mixing three entry types: automated playbook/task outputs, analyst free-text comments, and ad-hoc CLI command executions (any integration command can be run inline) — all timestamped, taggable, and audit-complete. It is explicitly described as "the first place to look when debugging a failing playbook."
- XSOAR separates transient investigation chatter from durable findings via an "Evidence Board": any War Room entry can be tagged and is then automatically promoted/pinned onto a separate, persistent Evidence Board for the incident — so the noisy live feed and the curated "what actually mattered" record are two different surfaces, not one long scroll.
- Incident-type-specific layouts are deterministic and admin-configured (not per-analyst whim): phishing incidents surface email metadata fields first, malware incidents surface sandbox verdict fields, account-compromise incidents surface privilege-change fields — the Incident Type field drives both the layout AND which playbook auto-fires. Layouts are built with a drag-drop widget/tab library (admin can add/reorder/hide tabs and fields, e.g. dragging "War Room" to appear after "Work Plan").
- XSIAM 3.0 (April 2025) introduced a "Cortex Command Center" as a single top-level unified dashboard across cloud + SOC: near real-time refresh (~30s cadence) of data sources/ingestion, asset inventory + posture, "pinpoint critical risks," and "monitor active attacks" — i.e., a command-center home page is now the vendor's own flagship pattern, not just an incident list.
- MITRE ATT&CK mapping is surfaced directly INSIDE the incident context (technique badges on the incident itself), in addition to a separate aggregate "MITRE ATT&CK Framework Coverage Dashboard" (top techniques across all open incidents) that exports/imports ATT&CK Navigator layers for coverage-gap visualization.
- Independent reviews (G2, PeerSpot) are consistently positive on detection/automation value but call out UI/UX as the weak point: "the UI/UX of the tool needs significant improvement, and parts of the tool run on an older framework, causing slowness"; "the dashboard experience could be improved." Even the category leader has real legacy-UI debt — this is a competitive opening, not just a risk to avoid.

**Concrete recommendations for us:**
- Build a 'Security Command Center' Overview (webui/src/soc/pages/Overview.tsx) around three zones mirroring XSIAM 3.0's Command Center, all backed by data we already compute: (1) Posture/sources health — reuse existing source health tiles + KpiTile; (2) Active risk — open cases by severity/status (RiskGauge + severity KPI drill-downs, already built); (3) Automation health — a new zone showing playbook/tool auto-actions taken today, HITL proposals pending, and the AutomationNudge recommendation. Keep the existing 60s visibility-aware refresh (already shipped in Round 6) as our analog to XSIAM's ~30s Command Center refresh cadence — don't add a new polling mechanism.
- Add a literal alert→case funnel/Sankey stat block to Overview: 'Events ingested → Correlated into cases → Auto-closed (FP) → Escalated (TP/needs_human)' with real counts from engine/metrics.py, explicitly labeling the FP-eliminated count the way PA's own collateral does ('X false positives auto-cleared this week'). This is MORE honest than PA's static customer-testimonial cards because ours is live, per-tenant data, not a marketing screenshot — lean into that as a differentiator.
- In webui/src/soc/pages/casedetail/WhyPanel.tsx, add a structured 'Risk factors' breakdown next to the existing ConfidenceBadge/decision_rationale text, modeled on SmartScore Explainability (SHAP-style): a small horizontal diverging bar list, one bar per contributing signal (e.g. '+ rare process on host', '+ known-bad IP reputation', '+ correlated with N other alerts', '− asset tagged as dev/sandbox'), sorted by magnitude, plain-language labels, positive=increases-risk (severity/danger token) vs negative=decreases-risk (success token) per the existing 3-axis severity/status/verdict palette. This turns the currently free-text-only decision_rationale into the same 'decomposable, inspectable score' pattern XSIAM ships — do NOT invent a new ML score; just expose the risk_score inputs engine/risk.py already computes as a labeled list.
- WhyPanel.tsx currently renders MITRE techniques as a flat row of badges (line ~314-320) while the richer MitreHeatmap component only exists on Metrics.tsx and the custom-dashboard widget registry (webui/src/soc/dashboard/widgets/mitre.tsx). Mirror XSIAM's practice of putting ATT&CK mapping directly in incident context: add a compact per-case variant of the heatmap (or at minimum tactic-grouped badges) to WhyPanel so an analyst sees 'this case = Initial Access + Execution' visually, not just a list of technique IDs.
- CaseThread (webui/src/soc/components/CaseThread.tsx) and its backing store (backend/app/stores/case_thread.py) currently have no 'pinned'/evidence concept at all (verified — zero matches). Add XSOAR's War Room→Evidence Board split: a `pinned: bool` field on thread entries + a 'Pin as evidence' action in CollaborationPanel.tsx, and render a compact 'Key evidence' strip at the top of the Collaboration tab that filters pinned=true — separating the noisy live discussion feed from the curated 'what actually mattered' record, exactly as XSOAR does. Zero new deps, zero new index (same KV store pattern this codebase already uses everywhere).
- Formalize the existing automation surfacing into an explicit three-lane 'Automation ledger' card at the TOP of CaseDetail (not buried in the TraceTimeline scroll), mirroring XSIAM's automatically-taken / requiring-review / recommended split: lane 1 = auto-applied actions (enrichment fetched, auto-close by decide(), tool calls at ToolTier.safe) with a check icon; lane 2 = 'waiting on you' (ToolTier.requires_approval calls queued, open HITL Proposals from threshold_tuning/threshold_automation) with an amber/pending token; lane 3 = 'suggested automation' reusing the existing AutomationNudge component/copy instead of duplicating it. This directly extends AutomationNudge rather than adding a new subsystem.
- Give the Trace/Executions tab (already TraceTimeline in CaseDetail.tsx) an explicit causality framing without adding a graph-visualization dependency: since this codebase enforces ZERO new npm deps, do NOT reach for react-flow/d3-force for a node-edge causality graph. Instead extend TraceTimeline with a lightweight 'why grouped together' inline annotation per step (e.g. a small badge on the correlation step reading 'linked to case-1042 via shared_ip 10.2.3.4') using existing chart/badge primitives — gets XSIAM's causality-view value without a new graph library.
- Add a durable 'automation impact' KPI tile to Overview ('N false positives auto-cleared, ~X analyst-hours saved this period') computed from case verdict counts + the usage/cost ledger, explicitly modeled on PA's own outcome-metrics framing (75% noise reduction / 270x MTTR / 10x closure rate). This is the single highest-leverage stat for winning SOC-manager buy-in and should sit above the fold on Overview, using the existing KpiTile delta-arrow component (Round 6 already fixed lower-is-better semantics — reuse it).
- Do not copy XSOAR's War Room CLI/ChatOps pattern of letting analysts run arbitrary ad-hoc integration commands inline — it is powerful for power users but is exactly the kind of feature reviewers flag as clutter/legacy-feeling on an older framework. Keep our simpler CaseThread (human/ai/system messages + reactions/tasks) and expose power-user tool actions as discrete labeled buttons (mapped to existing ToolTier-gated tools) rather than a freeform command line — preserves approachability while still surfacing the same capability.
- When building the Command Center / funnel, keep the 12 non-negotiables in view: any per-case 'why grouped' annotation or risk-factor label that surfaces log-derived/raw values MUST render through existing UNTRUSTED-fencing conventions (plain text / CodeBlock, per #9), exactly as WhyPanel.tsx already does for reasoning/knowledge snippets — new UI surfaces are the easiest place to accidentally reintroduce an unfenced untrusted string.

**Pitfalls to avoid:**
- Don't ship a bare numeric risk score with no breakdown — PA's own trajectory (SmartScore in XDR 3.4 → SmartScore Explainability two versions later) shows that an opaque AI score is a credibility problem serious enough that a market leader shipped a whole follow-on feature (SHAP-based factor breakdown) to fix it. Ship the factor breakdown from day one rather than retrofitting it.
- Don't conflate the live investigation feed with the durable findings record. XSOAR explicitly maintains two separate surfaces (War Room = everything, Evidence Board = curated subset) — cramming pinned/important findings into the same infinite-scroll thread as routine automation chatter (as our CaseThread currently does) makes the 'story' hard to reconstruct later.
- Don't bury automation status inside a generic activity/trace log. XSIAM treats 'what did automation already do / what's waiting on me / what should I automate next' as a first-class, always-visible summary, not something an analyst has to scroll a timeline to reconstruct.
- Don't add a graph-visualization library (react-flow, vis-network, cytoscape, d3-force, etc.) just to replicate XSIAM's Causality View node/edge graph — this codebase has a hard ZERO-new-runtime-deps discipline held across 6 rounds; a simpler annotated-timeline or swimlane approach delivers the same 'why were these linked' insight without a new dependency or bundle-size hit.
- Don't chase XSOAR's CLI/ChatOps War Room aesthetic for its own sake — independent reviews (G2/PeerSpot) show that even the category leader gets dinged for 'older framework' feel and dashboard UX; a dense command-line-in-a-timeline pattern is a plausible source of that complaint. Favor our existing clean, discrete-action button model.
- Don't put MITRE/ATT&CK mapping ONLY on an aggregate Metrics/coverage page. XSIAM surfaces technique badges directly on the incident itself as well as an aggregate coverage dashboard — an aggregate-only view (which is currently our state: MitreHeatmap exists only on Metrics.tsx / the dashboard widget registry, not on CaseDetail) loses the immediate 'why this case = this ATT&CK stage' context an analyst needs while triaging.
- Don't present noise-reduction metrics as a one-time marketing screenshot instead of a live, trustable number. PA's public numbers (75% reduction, 270x MTTR) are compelling but are static customer case-study snapshots frozen at a point in time — for a product whose whole pitch is auditable, trustworthy automation, the funnel/KPI band should be computed live from our own metrics engine and should degrade gracefully (empty state, not a fabricated demo number) when a tenant has little history, consistent with existing EmptyState conventions.

**Sources:**

## General SOC dashboard design best practices — information hierarchy, above-the-fold KPIs, the inverted pyramid, glanceability, and what belongs on a security overview (informing the Round-7 "Security Command Center" overhaul of webui/src/soc/pages/Overview.tsx)


**Key findings:**
- Nielsen Norman Group's classic eyetracking research (232 users, thousands of pages, replicated 11 years later) shows an F-shaped scan pattern: a long horizontal sweep across the top, a shorter second horizontal sweep lower down, then a vertical scan down the left edge — attention decays left-to-right and top-to-bottom. The single most important number on a dashboard belongs top-left with the largest size/highest contrast; secondary metrics go top-right; everything else decays in prominence going down and right. (nngroup.com/articles/f-shaped-pattern-reading-web-content-discovered/)
- Stephen Few's 'Information Dashboard Design' (the canonical dashboard-design text) defines a dashboard as data 'consolidated and arranged on a single screen so information can be monitored at a glance' — no scrolling to see what matters, aggressive removal of decoration (no 3D, no unnecessary chrome), and a hard requirement that a dashboard 'point out that something deserves attention and might require action.' (perceptualedge.com/files/Dashboard_Design_Course.pdf)
- NN/G's dashboard-specific guidance ('Dashboards: Making Charts and Graphs Easier to Understand') recommends preattentive-efficient encodings for quantitative comparison: 2D position and length (line charts, bar charts) are read almost instantly and accurately; area/angle encodings (pie/donut charts, gauges, tree-maps) are read slowly and inaccurately and should be avoided or demoted to decorative-only status; color should encode category, not quantity, and every color-coded meaning needs a redundant non-color cue (shape/label/icon) because ~8% of men are colorblind. (nngroup.com/articles/dashboards-preattentive/)
- NN/G distinguishes 'operational' dashboards (real-time, time-sensitive, drive an immediate decision — what a SOC overview IS) from 'analytical' dashboards (historical, exploratory). Operational dashboards should default to live/near-live data and a compressed set of metrics; deep exploration belongs on a separate analytics surface, not bolted onto the same screen.
- Miller's Law (Miller 1956, 'The Magical Number Seven, Plus or Minus Two') underlies the widely-cited dashboard rule of thumb: working memory reliably holds 5-9 discrete chunks; dashboards with >12 simultaneous KPIs show materially lower engagement/comprehension. This is the empirical basis for capping a KPI strip, not an arbitrary style preference. (en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Magical_Number_Seven,_Plus_or_Minus_Two)
- The 'inverted pyramid' (borrowed from journalism, now a named dashboard-IA pattern) structures a dashboard in three tiers: Tier 1 = outcome KPIs answering 'how are we doing right now' (top, largest, fewest); Tier 2 = driver/diagnostic metrics that explain the Tier-1 number's trend; Tier 3 = raw logs/lists/drill-in detail. Each tier should be reachable by progressive disclosure (click/expand), not all rendered at full weight simultaneously. (data.europa.eu/apps/data-visualisation-guide/the-inverted-pyramid)
- SANS 2025 SOC Survey (sponsored by Elastic, N=400+ SOC practitioners): 73% cite false positives as the #1 detection challenge (up sharply YoY); 69% of SOCs still compile SOC metrics manually and nearly half say it's too time-consuming; over 50% aren't even tracking MTTD/MTTR. The dominant complaint about SOC dashboards specifically is fragmentation — analysts can't get one consolidated view and have to stitch together multiple tools. This is direct evidence that 'one screen, one source of truth' is not a nicety but the #1 unmet need. (elastic.co/pdf/sans-soc-survey-2025.pdf)
- CrowdStrike's CrowdScore is the clearest industry precedent for a single 'hero number': instead of a wall of discrete alerts each carrying its own confidence score, related signals are fused into one incident-level score, and CrowdStrike reports a 98% reduction in the size of the analyst work queue (alert count → incident count) from this reframing alone. The UX lesson isn't the ML — it's that a SOC overview should foreground ONE authoritative aggregate risk/posture number, with everything else explaining or drilling into it. (crowdstrike.com/en-us/blog/crowdscore-solution-to-alert-fatigue/)
- Google Cloud Security Command Center's own design brief for its Overview page: 'first contact security dashboard, highlighting the highest priority risks... at-a-glance view of ongoing threats, ranked by severity' — i.e., the landing page's entire job is triage prioritization, not comprehensive reporting. (cloud.google.com/security-command-center/docs/assess-risk)
- Microsoft Sentinel workbook guidance converges on the same 5 filters nearly every SOC dashboard needs as global controls: time range (with quick 7d/30d presets), severity, status (open/closed), owner/assignee, and workspace/source scope — these should be first-class controls on the control bar, not buried in per-widget menus. (learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/sentinel/monitor-your-data)
- Emerging 2025-2026 research on explainable-AI SOC dashboards (arXiv 2503.02065 'Too Much to Trust?'; the 'Trust-Explainability curve' concept) argues explanation depth must be role-tiered: a Tier-1 triage view needs a one-line 'why' + confidence, while a Tier-3 hunter needs full reasoning traces — dumping full model rationale on the overview dashboard actively hurts glanceability and should be reserved for the case-detail drill-down, not the command-center landing page.

**Concrete recommendations for us:**
- Promote a single authoritative posture number into the truly-above-the-fold hero band. Today `webui/src/soc/pages/Overview.tsx` only puts the SLA-attainment chip in `<PageHeader meta=...>`; the 'Active Risk Index' (RiskGauge, 0-100) lives three rows down in the Zone-3 widget grid. Per the CrowdScore/Google-SCC precedent, add a second compact chip next to the SLA badge in the PageHeader `meta` slot — e.g. `Risk Index 62 (High)` using the same `scoreBand` palette token already used by RiskGauge/RiskBadge — so the one number that answers 'how are we doing right now' is visible with zero scroll on every viewport, not just above 900px of content.
- Reorder/cap the KPI strip to respect the F-pattern and Miller's Law. The strip is currently 7 tiles at `grid-cols-2 md:grid-cols-4 2xl:grid-cols-7` — that's at the edge of the 5-9 chunk budget, which is fine, but only if position encodes importance. Keep 'Open Cases' (already top-left, critical accent) as position 1; verify 'Critical/High' and 'Escalated To Human' (the two decision-relevant, action-now numbers) are positions 2-3, not interleaved with the less-urgent 'Total Cases'. Move 'Total Cases', 'Artifacts In Scope', and 'Knowledge Signals' (context, not action) to the back half of the strip or fold them under a details disclosure on narrow (<1280px) viewports where the strip wraps to 2 columns and pushes the widget grid further down.
- Make Row A (risk gauge + open-by-severity + attention queue) the guaranteed above-the-fold row and demote Row B/C. Test the current layout at 1366×768 and 1920×1080 (the two most common analyst-monitor resolutions) — hero + control bar + KPI strip + Row A should fit without vertical scroll; if it doesn't today, that's the Round-7 fix, not adding more content. Row B (autonomy split / timing trio / cost) and Row C (connector health / workload) are Tier-2/3 in inverted-pyramid terms and are correctly already lower, but consider collapsing Row C's two `DashboardGroup`s by default (the component already supports named collapsible bands per the file header comment) for first-time/narrow-viewport sessions, expanding only on click — classic progressive disclosure.
- Treat the RiskGauge arc as decorative reinforcement, never the sole encoding — it already does this correctly (big numeric `{score}/100` + `BAND_LABEL` text alongside the arc per the component's own a11y comment), so preserve that pattern; do NOT swap in a bare gauge/speedometer visual anywhere else on the overview (e.g., don't add a second gauge for SLA attainment or cost-budget — use a bar/line + big number instead, per NN/G's angle-encoding critique).
- Continue enforcing non-color-only severity signaling everywhere new is added: the existing `SEV_DOT` + `SEV_LABEL` + `SEV_BAR` triple (dot glyph, text label, and a length-based progress bar) in the severity widget is exactly the right pattern (redundant coding for the ~8% colorblind-affected population) — replicate this triple, not just a color chip, for any new severity-coded element (e.g., a future campaign-severity widget).
- Standardize the 5 global filters Sentinel/every mature SOC tool converges on: time range, severity, status, owner/assignee, source. `TimeRangePicker` + auto-refresh already covers time range in the `ControlBar`; severity/status/owner filters currently only exist inside the Cases list, not on Overview. Round-7 should NOT add full filter chips to Overview (that breaks glanceability/at-a-glance) — instead keep Overview read-only/summary and make every KPI tile and severity/workload row a one-click drill-through that PRE-APPLIES the matching severity/status/window filter on the Cases page (the code already does this via `navigate('cases', {...})` — extend the same pattern to any new tile rather than adding native filter controls to Overview itself).
- Add a per-tile trend indicator (up/down arrow + delta vs. prior window) using length/position, not color-only, consistent with `KpiTile`'s existing `goodDirection` prop (already correctly flips arrow-good/bad semantics for lower-is-better metrics like Open Cases/MTTR per the Round-6 fix) — extend this to the Row A/B widgets that don't yet show a delta (severity mix, connector health) so the overview answers not just 'what is the number' but 'is it getting better or worse,' which is the Tier-2 'driver metric' layer of the inverted pyramid.
- Resist scope creep into an analytical dashboard: per NN/G's operational-vs-analytical split, do not add exploratory/pivotable charts (drag-to-filter time series, arbitrary group-by) to Overview — that belongs on the existing Metrics/Analytics page (`webui/src/soc/pages/Metrics.tsx` / `Analytics.tsx`). Overview's job is exactly what Google SCC states for its Overview page: 'highlighting the highest priority risks... ranked by severity,' i.e., triage-prioritization at a glance, with every deeper need one click away on a dedicated page.
- Since SANS 2025 names fragmentation and manual metric-compilation as the #1 SOC dashboard pain point, make the `usePosture`/`getMetrics`/`usageSummary`/`ragStats` `Promise.allSettled` degrade-gracefully pattern (already implemented) a hard invariant for any new widget added in Round-7 — a single failing data source must never blank the whole page, only its own card (already true; keep it true as new widgets are added).

**Pitfalls to avoid:**
- Adding more KPI tiles because a new feature 'deserves visibility' — every tile added past ~9 measurably degrades comprehension (Miller's Law); the fix for a new important number is usually to REPLACE a lower-value tile or push it into a drill-down, not append a column.
- Using color as the only encoding for severity/status/verdict (fails ~8% of male users and fails on projectors/shared SOC-wall monitors with poor color rendering) — always pair with a text label or icon, as the codebase already does correctly; don't regress this in new widgets.
- Choosing a gauge/speedometer, pie, or donut chart for a 'looks impressive' hero visual — NN/G's preattentive-attribute research shows these are read slowly and inaccurately versus a big number or a length-based bar; if a gauge is kept for brand/identity reasons (as RiskGauge is), the numeric readout must dominate and the arc must be secondary, never the only value carrier.
- Building the overview as a scrollable analytics wall instead of a triage-first single screen — every widget added below the KPI strip should ask 'does this change what an analyst does in the next 5 minutes,' and if not, it belongs on Metrics/Analytics, not Overview.
- Reasoning traces / feature-attribution / full LLM rationale on the landing dashboard — explainability research (Trust-Explainability curve, arXiv 2503.02065) is explicit that dumping Tier-3-depth AI reasoning on a Tier-1 glanceable surface increases cognitive load without increasing trust; keep deep reasoning on the case-detail 'Why' tab (already built) and keep Overview to outcome-level numbers only.
- Adding native multi-select filter chips (severity/status/owner pickers) directly onto the Overview page — this converts an operational at-a-glance dashboard into a mini analytical/exploratory surface and violates the single-screen, minimal-interaction goal; prefer one-click drill-through navigation to Cases with the filter pre-applied (the existing pattern), not in-place filtering.
- Letting the KPI strip's F-pattern position be accidental (whatever order the code array happens to list) rather than deliberate — position 1 (top-left) must always be re-evaluated as features are added to make sure it's still the single most decision-critical number, not whatever was coded first.

**Sources:**

## Alert-fatigue / noise-reduction visualization for an agentic SOC dashboard — funnels vs. sankey vs. before/after, and which pattern best fits TLSOC's alert-to-case pipeline


**Key findings:**
- Vectra AI's Executive Overview report ships a literal 'Noise to Signal Funnel': Detections (all, excluding Info) -> entities with any detection -> prioritized alerts (after AI-Triage + manual triage rules + a prioritization threshold, RUX/QUX) -> final actioned signal. It is paired with a second chart, 'Noise to Signal Trends', directly beneath it (needs 2 months of data) so viewers see both the current funnel shape AND whether it's improving over time. (docs.vectra.ai)
- Expel Workbench's 'Alerts Analysis Dashboard' funnel is explicitly framed as: a large volume of signal goes in, gets analyzed in several stages, and the overall volume is reduced until the right signal is prioritized, analyzed, dispositioned, and acted upon. It sits alongside 'Workbench History', a chronological human-readable timeline of exactly what led an analyst to close-as-benign vs escalate. The funnel builds AGGREGATE trust; the timeline builds PER-CASE trust — Expel is the most-cited MDR example of this 'radical transparency' pairing.
- CrowdStrike Falcon Next-Gen SIEM ships native Sankey diagrams as a first-class dashboard visualization type, but the vendor's own examples use it for many-to-many/branching flows (vendor -> tactic -> technique), not a simple linear alert-to-incident count. This confirms the actual industry convention: reach for Sankey when there are multiple parallel paths/attribution to show, not for a single sequential drop.
- Elastic Security's alert view offers Summary/Trend/Counts/Treemap (grouped by up to 3 ECS fields). Treemap answers a composition question ('which rule/host/user is generating the noise') which is different from a funnel's sequential-flow question ('how much survives each stage') — vendors use different chart families for these two distinct questions rather than forcing one chart to do both.
- Splunk Enterprise Security leans on a disposition-tagged table (Incident Review / Notable Event framework) rather than a custom funnel chart at all — analysts tag a notable's disposition (true/false positive etc.) without changing its status, and simple aggregate counts over that tag do the 'how much was noise' job. This is evidence that a well-labeled table/stat-card set is a legitimate, lower-risk alternative to a bespoke chart.
- Marketing/exec reporting across the industry (Red Canary, UnderDefense, OpsRamp's 2025 survey) overwhelmingly favors a plain 3-5 number breadcrumb stat ('15,000 -> 2,000 alerts/day', 'up to 99% noise reduction', 'MTTR 4.5h -> 45m') over any chart for the executive glance — because a single delta number reads in under a second, while a full funnel/sankey is for the operational/analyst audience who need to know WHERE the volume went.
- Cross-vendor BI guidance (Atlassian's funnel-chart guide, Domo, Preset, growth-onomics) converges on hard rules: (1) funnel charts require strictly sequential, non-branching stages — if items can skip a stage or branch, a funnel actively misleads and Sankey is the correct tool instead; (2) 4-6 stages is the sweet spot, beyond ~8-10 stages visual differences become imperceptible and a table/waterfall communicates better; (3) bars must have STRAIGHT edges (not a wedge/trapezoid) so width alone, not area, encodes value — a trapezoid shape (the default in most funnel-chart libraries, including recharts' own <Funnel>) reintroduces the area-vs-value ambiguity that the straight-bar variant exists to avoid; (4) always label BOTH the absolute count and the step-over-step drop-off percentage on every stage (not just cumulative % of the top), because cumulative-only labeling hides exactly where the big win or the big problem is; (5) for Sankey, every node needs its value/percent labeled inline (never hover-only) and the number of visible flows should be limited to the ones that 'tell a story' — a fully-connected Sankey overwhelms.
- A concrete, zero-dependency, production-grade reference pattern exists for the primary funnel: shadcn/ui's 'Stats Funnel With Dropoff Rate' block renders each stage as a plain flex row with a rounded, PROPORTIONAL-WIDTH div (no charting library, no SVG path math), tabular-nums counts, and a per-row dropoff-percentage badge colored amber (moderate) vs red (severe) — this is exactly the straight-edge, always-labeled construction the BI guidance recommends, and it costs nothing but Tailwind classes.
- Alert-fatigue research (a 2025 ACM Computing Surveys paper on SOC alert fatigue; OpsRamp's 2025 survey: >10k alerts/day average, <5% require action, FP rates 50-80%) implicitly argues against a bare 'before vs after' number: a shrinking funnel can just as easily mean rising false-negatives (over-suppression) as genuine noise reduction, so any noise-reduction visualization needs to be co-located with a precision/agreement metric or it is unfalsifiable and reads as marketing rather than an operational signal.
- TLSOC-specific: the backend ALREADY implements a real, named, multi-stage noise-reduction pipeline that can be mapped 1:1 onto a funnel with zero invented stages: engine/ingest.py (raw OCSF events) -> engine/correlation.py cluster_from_events (clusters/candidates) -> [for EVENT-role feeds only] engine/event_detection.py's own documented 4-stage funnel (pre-aggregate -> rules -> anomaly-vs-baseline -> batched-LLM confirmation) -> engine/case_manager.py decide() producing Verdict (FALSE_POSITIVE/TRUE_POSITIVE/NEEDS_HUMAN) and CaseStatus/Disposition. Case-level stages (opened / auto-resolved FP / escalated) require ZERO new backend work since engine/metrics.py already aggregates verdict/status/disposition mixes and engine/shift_report.py already computes period-over-period deltas; pre-case stages (raw event count, cluster count) are currently NOT tracked as a stored metric anywhere in the codebase and would need a small new counter.
- recharts (already a dependency at ^2.15.4 in webui/package.json) ships BOTH a native <Sankey> component (node_modules/recharts/types/chart/Sankey.d.ts) and a native <Funnel>/<FunnelChart> (node_modules/recharts/types/chart/FunnelChart.d.ts, numberAxis/Funnel.d.ts) — so both a Sankey view and *a* funnel view are achievable with the project's stated zero-new-runtime-deps policy without adding anything; the caveat is recharts' default Funnel renders a trapezoid (wedge) shape, which the BI guidance above says is the wrong shape for an honest reduction story.

**Concrete recommendations for us:**
- Build the PRIMARY 'alerts -> cases' story as a hand-styled straight-edge bar funnel, not recharts' built-in <Funnel> (which renders a trapezoid/wedge and reintroduces area-vs-value ambiguity). New component webui/src/soc/components/NoiseFunnel.tsx: each stage is a flex row with a rounded-md div whose width = (stage_count / stage_0_count) * 100%, colored from the existing severity/status/verdict semantic token axes in palette.ts (not new hex), label on the left, tabular-nums count + a step-over-step dropoff-% badge (amber/red, matching the shadcn 'Stats Funnel With Dropoff Rate' reference pattern) on the right, and a small caption naming the CONTROL responsible for that drop (e.g. 'deduped by correlation', 'auto-closed by policy', 'confirmed by analyst'). Follow the existing charts.tsx a11y conventions: role="img" + aria-label per stage, SeriesGlyph/semanticIcon for non-color signaling (WCAG 1.4.1).
- Cap the default funnel at 5 stages (per the 4-6-stage BI rule): Raw Events -> Correlated Clusters -> Cases Opened -> Auto-Resolved (FALSE_POSITIVE, split-colored) vs Escalated (TRUE_POSITIVE/NEEDS_HUMAN, split-colored) as one bifurcated final stage -> optional Confirmed-TP (from analyst feedback). Push the EVENT-feed sub-funnel detail (event_detection.py's own pre-aggregate/rules/anomaly/batch-survivor stages) behind a 'View AI-detection breakdown' expand using the existing Sheet/Popover primitive rather than inlining 8+ stages into one chart.
- Backend: add engine/metrics.py::funnel_stats(cases, ingest_counters) -> dict, a pure aggregation function in the same style as the existing compute_metrics()/feedback_stats() (Counter over Verdict/CaseStatus/Disposition — all already on Case, zero new backend work for the case-level stages). Wire it as an ADDITIVE key on the existing GET /api/metrics response; do not add a new route.
- Backend: the pre-case stages (raw events ingested, clusters formed) are not currently tracked anywhere as a stored metric — add a small zero-migration rolling counter via a new stores/ingest_counters.py over the existing KVStore (same no-new-index/table pattern as stores/tuning.py and stores/campaigns.py), incremented once per accepted RawEvent in engine/ingest.py and once per cluster_from_events() call in engine/correlation.py, so Stage 1/2 of the funnel aren't left as DASH placeholders.
- Add a one-line headline breadcrumb ABOVE the full funnel using the existing PageHeader/KpiTile primitives: '1,284 raw -> 62 clusters -> 46 cases -> 3 escalated' with small arrow glyphs, clickable/anchor-linked to scroll to the full funnel below — this satisfies the '3-second exec glance' pattern every vendor uses (Red Canary/Expel/Vectra headline numbers) while keeping the operational detail one click away, and matches the Round-6 compact-hero (~52px PageHeader) precedent already established in this codebase.
- Directly beneath the funnel, reuse the EXISTING charts.tsx AreaChart/BarChart wrapper (already theme-token-driven, zero new code needed beyond a new data prop) to show the daily case-open count and daily auto-close rate over the shared TimeRangePicker window — this mirrors the Vectra 'funnel + trend chart beneath it' pairing and costs nothing extra since engine/metrics.py already computes a per-day case trend.
- Pin feedback_stats().agreement_rate (already computed, currently only surfaced elsewhere) as a small badge next to the funnel's 'Auto-Resolved' stage specifically, so a large FP-auto-close number is immediately checkable against analyst-confirmed agreement on the same screen — this directly defuses the ACM/OpsRamp-documented risk that a shrinking funnel is quietly suppressing real alerts rather than genuinely reducing noise.
- Reserve Sankey for a SEPARATE, opt-in 'case routing' view (not the primary noise-reduction story) — e.g. persona -> playbook -> verdict, or enrichment-source -> verdict — where genuine branching exists. Use recharts' native <Sankey> (already bundled in the existing dependency, no new package) rather than hand-rolling one; limit visible flows to the top N by volume per the 'only show flows that tell a story' guidance, and label every node's value inline rather than relying on hover tooltips (dashboards get screenshotted into reports).
- Segment the primary funnel by feed type using the existing SegmentedControl shared primitive: 'Realtime alerts' (the per-alert correlate -> decide path) vs 'Batched detection' (event_detection.py's pre-aggregate -> rules -> anomaly -> batch-LLM path) — these are two architecturally distinct pipelines in this codebase's own Round-4 two-tier ingestion design, and silently merging their counts into one bar would misattribute which control did the work.
- Keep all funnel-stage labels sourced from our own enums (Verdict/CaseStatus/Disposition) or fixed pipeline-stage names, which are safe to render as plain text; if a future stage label is ever derived from an operator-configured rule name or feed name (both source/operator-controlled), route it through the same plain-text-only rendering already used for case titles elsewhere in the codebase (never dangerouslySetInnerHTML) per non-negotiable #9.
- Verify the new component's stage colors against the existing design-gate test (WCAG-AA in both light and dark) rather than hardcoding new hex values — pull from the same severity/status/verdict token axes documented in theme.css so the funnel doesn't become the one component that breaks the project's measured-contrast baseline.

**Pitfalls to avoid:**
- Do not force a strictly sequential funnel onto a process that actually branches or lets items skip stages (e.g., a case that goes straight to escalation without passing through auto-triage) — per Atlassian/Domo/Preset guidance this is the single most-cited funnel-chart misuse and actively misleads; use a Sankey or a segmented/grouped bar for any path with real branching.
- Do not use a wedge/trapezoid funnel shape (recharts' own default <Funnel>, or most off-the-shelf funnel-chart libraries) for the headline reduction story — a trapezoid's visual AREA is not simply proportional to its value the way a straight rectangle's WIDTH is, so viewers systematically misjudge magnitude by area instead of width; use straight-edged bars instead.
- Do not label a funnel/Sankey with only cumulative '% of the top stage' numbers — always show the step-over-step drop-off too, or the dashboard hides exactly where a reduction (or a problem) is actually happening (correlation/dedup vs AI-triage vs analyst review each implies a different remediation).
- Do not rely on hover-only tooltips for stage/node values on either a funnel or a Sankey — SOC dashboards get screenshotted into reports and board decks; every stage's count must be visible inline without interaction.
- Do not cram more than ~6 funnel stages (or an unbounded number of Sankey nodes) into the primary view — beyond that, visual width/area differences become imperceptible per the BI guidance; collapse detail behind an expand/drawer instead of inlining everything.
- Do not present a single point-in-time 'before vs after' or '-99% noise' stat without a trend chart alongside it — a lone reduction percentage is unfalsifiable and reads as marketing; every best-in-class example found (Vectra explicitly) pairs the funnel with a trend-over-time chart directly beneath it.
- Do not let a shrinking alert/case funnel stand alone as a success metric — alert-fatigue research explicitly warns a shrinking funnel can mean rising false-negatives (over-suppression), not genuine noise reduction; always co-locate the funnel with a precision/agreement metric (this codebase already computes one) so the claim is checkable on the same screen.
- Do not merge two architecturally distinct pipelines (this product's realtime per-alert path and its batched EVENT-feed detection path) into one undifferentiated funnel — doing so misattributes which control absorbed which volume; segment by feed type instead.
- Do not add a new charting dependency for this feature — recharts (already at ^2.15.4) already ships both Funnel/FunnelChart and Sankey components, and the project's stated policy is zero new runtime deps; if you need a Sankey, use recharts' bundled one, and for the primary funnel prefer plain Tailwind rows (the shadcn reference pattern) over any chart-library funnel shape.

## Funnel chart design best practices for the TLSOC "Noise Reduction" alerts→cases funnel (proportional segments, labeling, conversion %, funnel-vs-bar/Sankey choice, accessibility)


**Key findings:**
- Proportionality is the most-violated rule: a tapered trapezoid/triangle funnel makes the visual AREA look like it encodes value, but only the width of the intersection line actually is proportional to the value — this 'area-width ambiguity' reliably causes readers to misjudge magnitude. Best-in-class tools (Amplitude, Mixpanel, most modern BI funnel widgets) instead use a 'bar-style' funnel: stacked rectangular horizontal bars whose WIDTH (a length encoding) is proportional to value, with no taper (inforiver.com, growth-onomics.com).
- Graphical-perception research (Cleveland & McGill's channel-accuracy ranking; Stevens' power law with an exponent of ~0.7 for area) shows position/length is decoded far more accurately than area, and that linearly-encoded areas are systematically UNDERESTIMATED — a tapered funnel doesn't just look different, it makes people wrong about magnitude in a predictable direction (arxiv.org/pdf/2001.02316, 'Surfacing Visualization Mirages').
- The 'funnel-neck problem': adding a rectangle/pill cap to the final (narrowest) stage to make it legible inflates its apparent area relative to its true value — a named failure mode that hits exactly the stage (final disposition: escalated/needs-human) a SOC noise-reduction funnel most needs to render honestly (inforiver.com).
- Converged labeling convention: each stage needs (1) stage name, (2) absolute count, (3) stage-to-stage conversion % (previous→this), placed directly ON the segment, never legend-only or hover-only. A cumulative/overall % (first stage→this stage) is shown as a secondary, more muted label. Sources agree the stage-to-stage number is the 'actionable' one ('38% of add-to-cart converts' beats '8% of all visitors') (growth-onomics.com, atlassian.com/data/charts/funnel-chart-complete-guide, docs.mixpanel.com/docs/reports/funnels).
- Amplitude's funnel bar splits each stage's own bar into a solid region (carried forward) and a striped/hatched region (dropped off at that step) — this dual-encodes 'stayed vs. left' inside ONE bar instead of needing a second chart, and the stripe pattern is a non-color signal that survives grayscale/colorblind rendering (amplitude.com/docs/analytics/charts/funnel-analysis).
- Optimal stage count converges on 4–6 (3–7 max) across sources; funnels with 10+ stages or with very small drop-offs between stages become visually imperceptible and should be collapsed or shown as a table instead (growth-onomics.com, domo.com/learn/charts/funnel-charts).
- Funnels are only valid for a STRICTLY sequential, monotonically-narrowing, single-path process; if users/records can skip stages, re-enter, branch, or merge, a funnel actively misleads and a Sankey diagram (link width ∝ flow volume) is the correct tool because 'the story is in the flow between stages, not the magnitude at each stage' (astrato.io/blog/sankey-use-cases, domo.com/learn/charts/sankey-diagrams).
- Sankeys have their own known failure mode — the 'spaghetti effect' — when there are too many nodes or many near-zero flows; that argues for keeping any flow-diagram alternative simple (2–4 node columns) rather than trying to show every source/connector in one diagram (astrato.io/blog/sankey-use-cases).
- 'Funnel plot' (statistics: effect-size vs. precision scatter used in meta-analysis) is an unrelated chart with the same name — a naming false-friend worth flagging so engineers don't pull the wrong library/example (en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Funnel_plot).
- Accessibility consensus across Datylon, Smashing Magazine (2024), and 216digital: (1) direct on-chart labels beat legends for both screen-reader and colorblind users; (2) WCAG requires ≥3:1 contrast between adjacent graphical elements/backgrounds (SC 1.4.11) and ≥4.5:1 for any text (SC 1.4.3); (3) color alone must never carry meaning (SC 1.4.1) — use patterns/textures/icons as a second channel; (4) every chart needs a text/tabular fallback (visible toggle or screen-reader-only) since a funnel's visual proportionality can't be conveyed by alt text alone; (5) static rendering is preferred over interaction-required rendering for keyboard/low-vision users, with interactivity as progressive enhancement only.
- Pattern/texture accessibility has concrete constraints: bars using a fill pattern should get a ~1px stroke outline and be rendered wide enough for the pattern to read; avoid more than ~5 light/dark stripe pairs in one element (photosensitivity risk) and prefer varied/wavy stripe weight over crisp high-frequency hatching (kerrykolosko.com/on-patterns-and-textures, smashingmagazine.com/2024/02/accessibility-standards-empower-better-chart-visual-design).
- Elastic/Kibana's own long-standing open issue on making charts screen-reader accessible (github.com/elastic/kibana/issues/11842) confirms this is an acknowledged, still-imperfect gap in the exact ecosystem (Elastic) this product sits next to — i.e. we cannot assume any inherited Kibana chart is already accessible and should not model our funnel on Kibana's default chart a11y.

**Concrete recommendations for us:**
- Build the Noise Reduction funnel as a custom bar-style (rectangular, non-tapered) component in webui/src/soc/components/charts.tsx — NOT a tapered SVG trapezoid and NOT a new charting-library funnel widget (Recharts FunnelChart / Nivo funnel). This keeps ZERO new npm deps (project convention) and gives width-as-length proportionality for free via flex/percentage widths, e.g. each stage row: `<div style={{ width: `${pct}%` }} className="h-11 rounded-md ...">` where pct = 100 * stageCount / firstStageCount.
- Pipeline stages, in fixed pipeline order (never reorder by size): Raw/Ingested Events → Correlated Alerts/Cases Opened → AI-Investigated → final Disposition row split into 3 verdict segments (Auto-Closed FP / Escalated TP / Needs Human) in one bar. That's 4 rows, inside the 4–6 sweet spot, and matches the actual engine/correlation.py → engine/risk.py → agents/investigator → engine/case_manager.decide() pipeline 1:1 so the chart is literally truthful to the code path (non-negotiable #3).
- Reuse the EXISTING measured-AA verdict tokens from Round 5 G1 (webui/src/soc/components/palette.ts: severity/status/verdict axes, each with token/-foreground/-text, already validated in both themes) for the final disposition segment colors — do not invent new hues for the funnel. Use a single neutral/desaturated 'in-pipeline' token (not a verdict color) for the earlier, not-yet-decided stages (raw events, alerts, AI-investigated).
- Explicitly do NOT use the conventional funnel red-for-drop-off / green-for-conversion metaphor. In this product, a LARGE auto-closed-FP segment is the desired, healthy majority outcome, not a loss — reusing the product's own false_positive/true_positive/needs_human verdict tokens (rather than a generic 'good=green/bad=red' funnel palette) avoids sending analysts the wrong signal that heavy auto-close is bad.
- On every stage row render three numbers, largest-to-smallest visual weight: stage name (bold), absolute count (bold), stage-to-stage % of the PREVIOUS row (medium weight, this is the 'actionable' one per research) and a muted small cumulative '% of total ingested' chip pinned to the row's right edge — mirrors the Atlassian/growth-onomics/Mixpanel convergence on showing both figures with stage-to-stage as primary.
- Split the final Disposition bar into 3 solid-color segments (FP/TP/Needs-Human) using the Amplitude 'solid vs textured' idea adapted: give the FP segment (the one 'leaving' the active pipeline) a diagonal-stripe SVG `<pattern>` fill in addition to its token color, and give the two segments that still need attention (Escalated, Needs Human) solid fills — this dual-encodes 'resolved-and-gone' vs 'still active' independent of color, satisfying WCAG 1.4.1 for exactly the distinction analysts care about.
- Enforce a MINIMUM rendered width floor (e.g. `Math.max(pct, 6)`%, or a fixed min-px) on every segment, with the true numeric label always rendered OUTSIDE/beside the bar (not clipped inside) when the segment falls below that floor. This is the single most SOC-specific fix needed: your funnel is meant to show 90%+ reduction, and a naive linear-width Escalated/Needs-Human segment (the very rows an analyst must act on) will otherwise render as an near-invisible sliver — exactly inverting the chart's priority.
- Never add a decorative end-cap, rounded pill, or extra rectangle to visually 'thicken' the final disposition row beyond its literal count-proportional width — this is the named 'funnel neck' failure mode and it would specifically distort the Escalated/Needs-Human read.
- Cap the funnel at 4 rows for the primary Command Center view. If a future need arises to compare noise-reduction across multiple sources/connectors, do NOT cram them into one funnel or force a single Sankey across all sources — render small-multiple funnels (one per source, reusing the same FunnelStage component) or a grouped bar of just the stage-to-stage % per source, per the Atlassian/Domo guidance against crowding.
- Reserve a Sankey (not a funnel) specifically for visualizing Round-4 campaign correlation (many cases sharing an entity graph merging into a Campaign) — that is a genuine many-to-one/branching flow, and forcing it into the strictly-linear Noise Reduction funnel would misrepresent it. If/when a campaign-flow view is built, keep it to 2–4 node columns to avoid the Sankey 'spaghetti effect'.
- Add a text/tabular fallback: a small 'View as table' toggle (reuse webui/src/ui/table, the existing shadcn Table primitive) that swaps the funnel for a real `<table>` with columns Stage | Count | % of previous | % of total — this is the standard 'every chart needs a tabular fallback' accessibility pattern and is cheap to build since the underlying data is already a flat array of {stage, count} the funnel consumes.
- Give the funnel's outer container `role="img"` and a computed `aria-label` summarizing it in one sentence (e.g. 'Noise reduction funnel: 10,432 raw events narrowed to 1,204 cases, an 88% reduction; of those, 76% auto-closed as false positive, 18% escalated, 6% needs human review') so screen-reader users get the takeaway without parsing bar geometry; set `aria-live="off"` on it given the dashboard's 60s auto-refresh (Round 6) so refreshes don't spam announcements.
- Validate segment-vs-background and segment-vs-segment contrast at ≥3:1 (WCAG 1.4.11, non-text) and any on-bar label text at ≥4.5:1 in BOTH themes — extend the repo's existing design-gate CI check (introduced in Round 5 G9 for the a11y/contrast work) to include the new funnel component's token-pair contrasts rather than eyeballing it once.
- Gate any stage-reveal / count-up animation on the funnel behind `prefers-reduced-motion`, reusing the existing Stagger/LoadingBar motion primitives already in webui/src/soc/components rather than adding new animation code — keeps the funnel static-by-default per the 'static beats interactive' accessibility guidance while still getting the existing polish for users who want it.
- Keep the funnel itself non-interactive as the source of truth (no hover-only data, no click-required drill-down for the core numbers); optional click-to-filter-cases-by-stage is fine as progressive enhancement layered on top, not as the only way to read the numbers.

**Pitfalls to avoid:**
- Do not build a tapered trapezoid/triangle SVG funnel — area-width ambiguity plus Stevens'-law area underestimation means readers will misjudge exactly the drop-off magnitude the chart exists to communicate.
- Do not add a rectangle/pill/cap to visually enlarge the final (narrowest, most important) stage — the 'funnel neck' problem inflates perceived importance-adjacent area without changing the number.
- Do not encode 'auto-closed vs escalated vs needs-human' with color alone (especially not a generic red/green good/bad funnel palette) — it both fails WCAG 1.4.1 and sends an inverted signal in this product (large auto-close = healthy, not a loss).
- Do not let small-but-critical segments (Escalated, Needs Human) shrink to sub-pixel/illegible widths under naive linear scaling — enforce a minimum visible width + always-external label.
- Do not exceed ~4–6 stages in one funnel, and do not try to cram multiple sources/connectors into a single funnel or a single giant Sankey — use small multiples instead.
- Do not confuse a statistical 'funnel plot' (meta-analysis effect-size-vs-precision scatter) with this UI funnel chart when researching or picking a library/example — different chart, same name.
- Do not rely on hover tooltips or a legend as the only place stage name/count/% appear — label directly on/beside each segment for both scanning speed and accessibility.
- Do not pull in Recharts/Nivo/D3 funnel packages just for this — it's a 1400 kB+ dependency footprint (and a new-dep decision the CLAUDE.md conventions gate) for something a flex/percentage-width div plus one SVG stripe `<pattern>` already solves.
- Do not use a strict funnel for genuinely branching/merging flows (e.g. campaign correlation, multi-source re-entry, case reopen/bounce) — that's the case where funnels are actively misleading and a Sankey (kept small, 2-4 columns) is the honest tool.

**Sources:**
- https://inforiver.com/insights/funnel-charts-how-and-when-to-use-them/
- https://growth-onomics.com/best-practices-for-minimalist-funnel-charts/
- https://www.atlassian.com/data/charts/funnel-chart-complete-guide
- https://www.domo.com/learn/charts/funnel-charts
- https://www.astrato.io/blog/sankey-use-cases
- https://www.domo.com/learn/charts/sankey-diagrams
- https://www.datylon.com/blog/data-visualization-for-visually-impaired-users
- https://www.smashingmagazine.com/2024/02/accessibility-standards-empower-better-chart-visual-design/
- https://216digital.com/creating-accessible-data-for-charts-and-graphs/
- https://www.w3.org/WAI/ARIA/apg/patterns/table/
- https://kerrykolosko.com/on-patterns-and-textures/
- https://github.com/elastic/kibana/issues/11842

- https://amplitude.com/docs/analytics/charts/funnel-analysis/funnel-analysis-how-amplitude-computes-conversions

- https://docs.mixpanel.com/docs/reports/funnels/funnels-faq
