## Command-center visual language for a SOC console — dark UI, glass/blur, glow, and grid overlays that read as authoritative and premium without becoming gimmicky or hurting readability


**Key findings:**
- Nielsen Norman Group and Apple's Human Interface Guidelines agree glass/blur ('materials') only work when (a) confined to CHROME the designer controls the background of (headers, sheets, palettes, popovers) and (b) blur is strong enough to fully obscure the busy content behind it — weak/low blur over an intricate background is the single most common glassmorphism failure. Apple frames vibrancy/blur as something to apply with restraint and test across contexts, not a default surface treatment. (nngroup.com/articles/glassmorphism, developer.apple.com/design/human-interface-guidelines/materials)
- Accessibility guidance (Axess Lab, WCAG 2.2) gives a concrete blur ceiling: avoid `backdrop-filter: blur()` values of ~20px and above — beyond that you're fighting contrast, not aiding depth. It also mandates two CSS escape hatches: `@media (prefers-reduced-transparency: reduce)` (drop to opaque) and `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)` (kill shimmer/pulse), with a semi-opaque solid fill + hairline border as the fallback so hierarchy survives when blur is stripped.
- Material Design's dark-theme spec: never use pure black (#000000) as the base surface — it's visually harsh and paradoxically lowers legibility over long sessions; use near-black gray (Material's baseline is #121212). Elevation in dark mode is shown by LIGHTENING a surface as it rises (an overlay wash), not by darker drop-shadows the way light mode does — shadows barely register on black. Foreground text must hold >=4.5:1 against every elevation tier; fully-saturated colors optically 'vibrate' on a dark field so lighter/desaturated fills read better. (m3.material.io/blog/android-dark-theme-tutorial, Google Codelabs dark-theme guide)
- Linear (the reference for 'premium but restrained' dark SaaS UI) is dark-FIRST, not dark-as-inversion: every surface/elevation/accent was tuned in LCH color space starting from the dark theme, light mode derived second. Depth cues come from very small lightness deltas between adjacent surfaces (a dropdown reads 'closer to the light source' than the page behind it) rather than heavy borders or glow; chrome/blue is deliberately rationed so the UI doesn't skew cold/cluttered. (linear.app/now/how-we-redesigned-the-linear-ui, chyshkala.com analysis)
- Real command-center precedent (Bloomberg Terminal, enterprise NOC video walls) does NOT chase a sci-fi look. Authority comes from information density, predictability and speed — dense grids, monospaced/tabular figures, a small deliberate palette used consistently for meaning (not decoration). NOC literature is explicit that a wall only earns trust if analysts can name, at a glance, which of ~4 fixed layers (health / alarms-SIEM / ticket-escalation state / context) they're looking at — decoration that doesn't map to a layer becomes 'wallpaper, not signal.' (bloomberg.com/company/stories/how-bloomberg-terminal-ux-designers-conceal-complexity, extnoc.com/network-operations-center/noc-design-and-layout)
- SOC-specific UX research names the failure mode directly as 'alarm fatigue': one cybersecurity-dashboard-trust write-up found analysts built an ALTERNATE severity palette (still 4 tiers, but hues chosen to separate 'visual urgency' from 'emotional alarm') because 8-12 hour shifts under a stock red/amber/green scheme produce cumulative stress that erodes trust in the tool. 'When everything is urgent, nothing is urgent' — red should be reserved for the small set of states needing immediate action. (theskinsfactory.com/uiux-design-blog/cybersecurity-ux-design-trust, ACM Computing Surveys 'Alert Fatigue in SOCs')
- Glow, done right, is a STATE signal, not ambient bloom: a colored `box-shadow` ring (0-offset, moderate blur, semantic token) on the ONE element needing attention (a live/streaming badge, a just-escalated row, an active session dot) — never a page-wide aura. Animate `opacity`/`transform` on a pseudo-element that already carries the glow rather than animating the shadow's blur-radius/color directly, since that isn't GPU-composited and will jank on lower-end hardware. (freefrontend.com/css-glow-effects)
- `backdrop-filter: blur()` is measurably GPU/compositor-expensive — it recomputes everything behind the element on every visible/scrolling frame, which is why shadcn/ui has open perf issues about it. Budget it: a handful of simultaneously-blurred chrome panels, never blur behind a scrolling data table or a live-updating list. (github.com/shadcn-ui/ui/issues/327)
- Subtle grid/'engineering precision' overlays are cheap and well-worn: two `repeating-linear-gradient`/`linear-gradient` layers at low opacity (1px lines, ~24-32px pitch), zero HTTP cost, fully themeable via CSS custom properties — exactly the shape of the `.command-grid::before` rule already in this codebase. The same family (subtle noise/grain at 5-15% opacity) is the accepted way to keep a flat dark canvas from looking 'sterile' without reading as decoration.
- This codebase (webui/src/styles/theme.css, webui/src/soc/theme.tsx, webui/src/soc/components/GlassSurface.tsx) already encodes almost all of the above as an explicit, tokenized, OPT-IN 'material pack' system: a 'quiet' default (glass-opacity 0.82/0.78, glow-strength 0, grid-opacity 0 — command-center chrome OFF by default) versus an admin-selectable 'command' pack that raises `--glow-strength`/`--grid-opacity`; GlassSurface is explicitly documented CHROME-ONLY ('DO NOT use... behind data tables, long-form text, or dense dashboards'); the grid overlay is aria-hidden, opacity-token-driven, and radially masked to fade out; and a `prefers-reduced-transparency: reduce` block already zeroes glass/glow/grid and strips `backdrop-filter`, `!important`-guarded against inline branding overrides. This is a stronger baseline than most current design-trend discourse — remaining work is tuning/extending it, not inventing it.

**Concrete recommendations for us:**
- Codify a rule the team can review by eye: 'glass/glow/grid is chrome-and-signal only, never fact-bearing surface.' Concretely — GlassSurface (webui/src/soc/components/GlassSurface.tsx) stays reserved for AppShell header, Sheet/Dialog, command palette, hover popovers; every place rendering SIEM-asserted facts (raw event fields, the case Timeline/Trace, the 'Why' rationale tab, DataTable rows) must render on a flat, fully-opaque bg-card/bg-surface. Use presence-of-blur itself as an epistemic cue for your stated product goal: AI-derived narrative/assessment can live in a GlassSurface floating card; SIEM-asserted fact always renders flat/opaque with higher local contrast.
- Audit current GlassSurface call sites for the 'lg' (22px) blur preset against the ~20px accessibility ceiling (Axess Lab); either drop 'lg' to ~16-18px or restrict it to contexts with a guaranteed plain backdrop (e.g. AppShell header over the flat canvas color, never over an image/gradient hero). Keep 'sm'(8px)/'md'(14px) as-is.
- Extend the existing `@media (prefers-reduced-transparency: reduce)` block in theme.css to ALSO key off `@media (prefers-contrast: more)` with the same reset (--glass-opacity:1, --glow-strength:0, --grid-opacity:0, backdrop-filter:none) — a user requesting more contrast has the identical need as one requesting less transparency, and today only the transparency query is wired.
- Ship the 'command' material pack as an explicit, documented, opt-in toggle framed for a use case in Settings/Branding copy: 'Command mode — for shared displays / SOC wall screens' vs default 'Quiet — for analyst desks and long shifts.' This matches both the existing architecture (material defaults to 'quiet' in soc/theme.tsx) and the NOC/fatigue research: a wall-mounted overview screen can afford glow/grid; a desk analyst staring at cases for 8+ hours should default calm. Don't let 'command' become default just because it demos better.
- For the Security Command Center dashboard's KPI/severity strip: keep the existing 3-axis token system (severity/status/verdict, Okabe-Ito ramp, non-color SEMANTIC_ICON) but audit that red/critical is reserved for states truly needing immediate action (open TRUE_POSITIVE/ESCALATED counts), not every 'high' bucket. If more than ~10-15% of visible tiles are red at any time, analysts habituate and it stops functioning as signal.
- For the 'Noise Reduction' alerts-to-cases funnel infographic: render each stage as a flat rectangle (no bevel/gradient/glow) sized by data (bar length or opacity fade, not hue) so volume reduction communicates through geometry; reserve color for the one or two stages that are actually risk-bearing (e.g. 'escalated' in --critical, everything upstream neutral/muted) rather than a traffic-light gradient across every stage.
- Don't stack the grid overlay AND a noise/grain texture by default on large canvas areas (Overview, login hero) — pick one ambient signature and gate it behind the SAME opacity-token pattern already used for `.command-grid`, zero in 'quiet', present only in 'command'.
- Restrict animated glow to true LIVE states — the SSE-connected 'live' badge, a just-escalated case row for ~2s, an active session dot — never a permanently-pulsing decorative accent. Animate via opacity/transform keyframes on a ::before pseudo-element carrying the box-shadow (not the shadow's blur/spread), gated behind the existing global prefers-reduced-motion rule.
- Extend the existing Inter (UI) / JetBrains Mono (technical) typographic split to every place raw SIEM facts render (timestamps, IPs, hashes, funnel/KPI counts) with tabular figures — a visible typographic distinction between 'literal field value' (mono) and 'prose/AI narrative' (Inter) reinforces the facts-vs-assessment separation goal.
- Cap the number of simultaneously-mounted backdrop-filter elements per view (header + at most one open Sheet/palette) and never mount GlassSurface behind/around a scrolling DataTable or live-tail log viewer. Turn the existing doc-comment rule into an enforced check (grep-based CI or Storybook rule flagging GlassSurface nested inside table/list containers).
- Keep the `.command-grid::before` grid line effective opacity conservative (roughly 0.03-0.05 at the 'command' pack's default strength) — a grid you have to notice to see is doing its job; one you notice immediately reads as decorative/sci-fi.

**Pitfalls to avoid:**
- Sci-fi/HUD aesthetic transfer — clipped-corner panels, radar-sweep animations, reticle/scan-line motifs, neon multi-color glows. Search results for 'HUD sci-fi command center UI' return almost entirely After-Effects/CodePen novelty content with zero enterprise-software adoption — it signals 'demo reel,' not 'audited security tool.'
- Glassmorphism over data-bearing or scrolling surfaces (tables, log viewers, case Timeline) — contrast becomes background-dependent exactly where an analyst needs to trust every character (a misread IP/hash in a security tool is a real failure, not a cosmetic one).
- Blur values >=20px used liberally, or low blur over busy/colorful backgrounds — both called out as the common glassmorphism failure mode by NN/g and Axess Lab.
- Pure black (#000000) backgrounds — reads as harsh/fatiguing over long shifts; Material's own guidance and multiple dark-theme writeups converge on near-black gray instead.
- Full-saturation red/yellow/green traffic-light severity applied indiscriminately across every tile/badge — produces alarm fatigue and habituation; SOC-specific research found teams deliberately breaking this convention for that exact reason.
- Decorative glow/grid/noise not gated behind prefers-reduced-motion / prefers-reduced-transparency / prefers-contrast — an accessibility regression and a sign the effect was added for its own sake.
- Animating box-shadow blur-radius or color directly for pulsing glow — not GPU-composited, causes jank especially on lower-end hardware exactly when a machine is already busy with a live SSE stream or heavy table.
- Stacking multiple ambient textures (grid overlay + noise grain + gradient mesh + glow) simultaneously 'for richness' — they compound into visual noise and undermine the clean/authoritative goal; pick one restrained ambient signature gated behind a single opt-in toggle.
- Treating 'premium' as 'more effects' rather than 'more restraint' — the strongest cross-source signal (Linear, Bloomberg, NOC literature, Apple HIG) is that authority comes from information density, predictability and a small deliberately-used palette, not additional chrome; an over-decorated security tool reads as unserious just as fast as an unpolished one erodes trust.
- Letting the 'command' material pack (glow/grid) become the default just because it demos better — dramatic command-center chrome is appropriate for shared/wall displays, not an analyst's daily 8-hour desk view; keep 'quiet' the shipped default.

**Sources:**
- https://www.nngroup.com/articles/glassmorphism/
- https://axesslab.com/glassmorphism-meets-accessibility-can-frosted-glass-be-inclusive/
- https://developer.apple.com/design/human-interface-guidelines/materials
- https://m3.material.io/blog/android-dark-theme-tutorial
- https://codelabs.developers.google.com/codelabs/design-material-darktheme

## Explaining metrics in-product — tooltip/help (?) affordance patterns for formula disclosure (MTTR, risk index) with progressive disclosure, for the TLSOC Round-7 UI overhaul


**Key findings:**
- Progressive disclosure's one hard rule (Nielsen/NN-g): the primary view must carry everything users need FREQUENTLY; the formula/methodology behind a metric is secondary and reached via a clearly-labelled, low-friction control — never inline text competing with the number itself, and never buried more than one click deep. nngroup.com/articles/progressive-disclosure
- Every enterprise design system that documents this (IBM Carbon, PatternFly/Red Hat — both IT-ops/security-adjacent) draws the same hard line: Tooltip = hover/focus-triggered, ~1 sentence, identification only, NOT interactive, no links, no formulas. Popover = click-triggered, can hold multiple sentences + formatted text + a code/formula block + a 'Learn more' link, dismissed explicitly (Esc / click-outside / close icon). Rule of thumb: tooltip answers 'what is this', popover answers 'how was this calculated / why'. carbondesignsystem.com/components/popover, patternfly.org/components/popover/design-guidelines
- Grafana's own design system (the closest sibling product — observability dashboards with the identical MTTR/percentile-tile problem) restricts Tooltip content to concise, non-critical, NON-interactive text, and explicitly directs anything richer/interactive to a click-triggered 'ToggleTip' instead of a hover Tooltip. grafana.com/developers/saga/components/tooltip
- Datadog's production pattern for 'what does this number mean' is a 2-3 tier reveal: an info icon in the widget header opens a short description; deeper metadata (units/type/tags/formula) is one more click away via a context menu — i.e., icon → short description → full detail, never all three at once. docs.datadoghq.com/dashboards/widgets/configuration
- Elastic Security's entity risk score (the closest analog to our 'Active Risk Index' — an aggregated weighted score with contributing inputs) does NOT put the formula in a tooltip at all. It shows the score, then a 'View risk contributions' expander revealing per-input contribution (alerts, asset criticality, watchlists) as a ranked breakdown, plus an optional AI-assistant 'explain this score' action. The lesson: for a COMPOSITE/weighted score, a static formula tooltip is less useful than an itemized contribution breakdown — disclose the RECIPE (what fed the number) not just the equation. elastic.co/docs/solutions/security/advanced-entity-analytics/entity-risk-scoring, elastic.co/docs/solutions/security/advanced-entity-analytics/view-and-analyze-risk-score-data
- Stripe Radar's risk-factor UI uses the same layered pattern for a similarly opaque ML-driven score: top factors shown inline with a small multiplier badge (e.g. '3.5x'), a 'Show all insights' action opens a dialog with the full ranked list of contributing factors — again itemized contribution over raw formula. docs.stripe.com/radar/reviews/risk-insights
- AI/agentic-product confidence-visualization guidance converges on: show the qualitative confidence level + color first, then progressively reveal WHAT drove the confidence (data quality, corroborating signals, historical calibration) on request; avoid false precision (do not show 87.34%, prefer 'High' + a plain-language driver list). This maps directly onto our AI-derived verdict/confidence chips, which are a sibling case to MTTR/risk-index disclosure. agentic-design.ai/patterns/ui-ux-patterns/confidence-visualization-patterns
- WCAG 2.2 SC 1.4.13 (Content on Hover or Focus, Level AA) legally/structurally constrains ANY hover tooltip used for real information (not just decoration): it must be dismissible without moving the pointer (Esc), hoverable (pointer can move onto the tooltip content itself without it vanishing), and persistent (stays until the user dismisses it or moves focus away, not on a timeout). A plain title-attribute or a tooltip that vanishes the instant the cursor leaves the trigger fails this. w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/content-on-hover-or-focus.html
- Tooltip AND HoverCard are both fundamentally hover/focus-only primitives that do not work reliably on touch devices (no hover state) — multiple open Radix/shadcn issues confirm this is an unresolved architectural gap, not a bug. Any affordance an analyst genuinely NEEDS (not just a nicety) should be click-triggered (Popover/Dialog), which also transfers focus and announces to assistive tech, rather than hover-only. github.com/radix-ui/primitives/issues/2278, radix-ui.com/primitives/docs/components/tooltip
- OUR OWN CODEBASE ALREADY HAS THE RIGHT PRIMITIVE, just not applied to metrics: `webui/src/soc/components/HelpTip.tsx` implements exactly the Carbon/Grafana-recommended split — renders a Radix Tooltip for short (<80 char, no link/code) help text, and automatically upgrades to a Radix Popover (with an optional formatted code block + 'Learn more' link) when the text is long or a link/code is supplied. It is currently wired into form fields, connector auth fields, notification templates, and the rule editor/lifecycle sheet — but is NOT used anywhere on `KpiTile.tsx`, `RiskGauge.tsx`, `DashboardGroup.tsx`, `Metrics.tsx`, or `Overview.tsx`, i.e. exactly the surfaces that render MTTA/MTTR/dwell/Active-Risk-Index with zero explanatory affordance today (confirmed by reading the files: KpiTileProps has no help/tooltip prop, RiskGauge has no help prop, the 'Active Risk Index' DashboardGroup only carries a static `description` string 'weighted pressure').

**Concrete recommendations for us:**
- Add an optional `help?: { text: string; link?: string; code?: string }` prop to `KpiTile` (webui/src/soc/components/KpiTile.tsx) and render a `<HelpTip {...help} label={`About ${label}`} className="ml-1" />` immediately after the `label` span in the header row (next to the existing icon chip). This is additive/backward-compatible — no existing call site breaks — and reuses the tooltip/popover split HelpTip already implements, so no new component or dependency is needed.
- In `Metrics.tsx`'s `lifecycleTiles` array (around line 920) and in `Overview.tsx`'s response-timing block (around line 366-383), pass `help` text per metric, e.g. MTTA: 'Mean Time to Acknowledge — time from case creation to an analyst first acknowledging it (status → INVESTIGATING). p50 = median across cases in the selected window.'; MTTR: 'Mean Time to Resolve — time from case creation to RESOLVED/CLOSED. p50 = median.'; Dwell/MTTD: 'Time the underlying activity was present in logs before TLSOC first raised a case.' Pull the exact definition/window semantics from `backend/app/engine/metrics.py` and `engine/shift_report.py` so the UI text is byte-true to what the backend actually computes (avoid re-describing a formula the backend doesn't implement) — e.g. Round-4/5 notes explicitly log 'honest labelled DASH' for insufficient-sample cases, so the tooltip should also state that a dash means insufficient data, not zero.
- Add the same optional `help` prop to `RiskGauge` (webui/src/soc/components/RiskGauge.tsx) and render a `HelpTip` beside the band label (the `BAND_LABEL[band]` span, ~line 157) OR beside the external `label` line below the gauge. For the 'Active Risk Index' specifically, do NOT just describe a formula in a tooltip — follow the Elastic/Stripe pattern and add a click-through breakdown: extend the popover (or add a 'View contributions' link inside it, using the existing `link` prop pattern or a new `onExplain?: () => void` prop) that opens a small sheet/dialog listing the top contributing cases/signatures and their weight, sourced from whatever backend aggregate already feeds the gauge (check `engine/risk.py` / the Overview `usePosture`/risk-index endpoint for a per-case or per-signature breakdown to reuse — do not compute a new one client-side).
- Add `help` to `DashboardGroup` (webui/src/soc/components/DashboardGroup.tsx): today `description` is a plain muted sub-line string with no explanatory affordance. Add an optional `help?: HelpTipProps` prop rendered as a `HelpTip` next to the `Heading`, for group titles like 'Active Risk Index' where the sub-line ('weighted pressure') is itself unexplained jargon — this is the single line users most need explained, per the NN/g rule that progressive disclosure exists precisely for compressed/jargon labels like this.
- For any AI-derived value (verdict confidence, priority/urgency chips, campaign/tuning scores) that is NOT a fixed arithmetic formula but a model-derived judgement, do not present a fake precise 'formula' — follow the confidence-visualization convergence: keep the qualitative label/band as primary, and put the DRIVERS (which signals/enrichment/playbook fed the verdict) behind the HelpTip popover or link straight into the existing `/api/cases/{id}/rationale` 'Why' tab, which already exists in this codebase (`CaseDetail`'s Why tab, engine 'decision_rationale') — i.e. the metric-help affordance on a case's confidence chip should deep-link to the Why tab rather than duplicate its content in a tooltip.
- Always use the Popover branch of HelpTip (never force the short-tooltip branch) for anything an analyst might need on a touchscreen/tablet triage station or that contains a formula/link — per the WCAG 1.4.13 + Radix touch-support findings, hover-only tooltips are inaccessible on touch and risk failing dismissible/hoverable/persistent if a future refactor swaps primitives. Concretely: when adding `help` text for MTTR/risk-index, prefer text length/content that naturally trips HelpTip's existing `usePopover` threshold (>80 chars, or supply a `link`) rather than relying on the short hover-tooltip path, so these financially/operationally significant metrics always get the click-accessible, WCAG-compliant surface.
- Add ONE canonical source of these metric definitions (e.g. `webui/src/soc/lib/metricGlossary.ts` exporting `{ mtta, mttr, dwell, riskIndex, mtti, ... }` help-text objects) so `Overview.tsx`, `Metrics.tsx`, and any future dashboard widget reference the SAME wording instead of hand-typed duplicate strings drifting apart — this mirrors the Datadog/Grafana pattern of one metric-metadata registry feeding every widget's info affordance, and keeps the eventual per-user custom Dashboards (Round-5 G7 widget registry, `soc/dashboard/*`) automatically correct when a widget for MTTR is dropped onto a custom dashboard.
- For power users, consider a lightweight third tier consistent with the existing IA: a `GET /api/metrics` or static 'Definitions' anchor within the existing Metrics page (a collapsible `DashboardGroup` titled 'How these numbers are calculated') that lists every glossary entry in one place, so the popover's 'Learn more' link (HelpTip already supports `link`) can point in-app to `#definitions` instead of an external URL — avoids introducing a new /docs surface while still giving the 'go read the whole spec' tier progressive disclosure calls for.
- Keep HelpTip's existing WCAG 2.5.8-sized 24px hit target and `aria-label` pattern (already correct) when wiring it into KpiTile/RiskGauge; do not wrap the whole tile in a tooltip trigger (that would make the entire KPI card's click target ambiguous with KpiTile's own `onClick` drill-down behavior) — keep the HelpTip a small, separate, independently-focusable icon so the KPI tile's existing click-to-drill-down affordance and the new click-to-explain affordance never compete for the same click target.

**Pitfalls to avoid:**
- Do not put a formula or link inside a hover-only Tooltip — Grafana/Carbon/PatternFly all treat that as a Popover's job; a Tooltip with a link or multi-sentence formula breaks the established tooltip/popover contract users already have from other enterprise tools and often breaks WCAG 1.4.13 (vanishes before the user can read/click it).
- Do not show a fake-precise formula for a fundamentally ML/LLM-derived value (verdict confidence, priority) — the AI-UX literature explicitly warns against false precision (e.g. '87.34% confidence'); show a qualitative band plus driver list instead, consistent with how this product already treats risk bands (RiskGauge shows a word band, not just a raw number).
- Do not bury the explanatory affordance more than one click deep from the metric itself — NN/g's core progressive-disclosure failure mode is forcing users to hunt (e.g., a separate Settings/Help page) for a definition of a number they're staring at; the (?) icon must sit directly beside the metric.
- Do not rely on a hover-only tooltip for anything on a touch/tablet SOC triage station, and do not implement a tooltip that disappears when the pointer moves toward it (fails the WCAG 'hoverable' sub-requirement) — this is a known, currently-unresolved Radix/shadcn limitation, so lean on the click-triggered Popover path for anything substantive rather than fighting the hover primitive.
- Do not duplicate metric definition text ad hoc at each call site — wording will drift (e.g., MTTR described one way on Overview and another on Metrics), which erodes trust in a product whose entire value proposition is auditable, explainable numbers; centralize the glossary strings.
- For a COMPOSITE/weighted score like the Active Risk Index, do not stop at a static formula tooltip — best-in-class competitors (Elastic entity risk, Stripe Radar) all show a per-input CONTRIBUTION breakdown, which is strictly more useful and directly reusable in this codebase since the backend already computes and audits component signals feeding risk/priority (engine/risk.py, engine/priority.py) — a text-only formula tooltip would be a downgrade from what the backend can already justify.
- Do not let the new help affordance's click target overlap or compete with an existing interactive affordance on the same component (e.g. KpiTile's own onClick drill-down) — keep HelpTip a small, independently focusable trigger so keyboard/screen-reader users can reach both actions unambiguously.

**Sources:**
- https://www.nngroup.com/articles/progressive-disclosure/
- https://carbondesignsystem.com/components/popover/usage/
- https://www.patternfly.org/components/popover/design-guidelines/
- https://grafana.com/developers/saga/components/tooltip/
- https://docs.datadoghq.com/dashboards/widgets/configuration/
- https://www.elastic.co/docs/solutions/security/advanced-entity-analytics/entity-risk-scoring
- https://www.elastic.co/docs/solutions/security/advanced-entity-analytics/view-analyze-risk-score-data
- https://docs.stripe.com/radar/reviews/risk-insights
- https://agentic-design.ai/patterns/ui-ux-patterns/confidence-visualization-patterns
- https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/content-on-hover-or-focus.html
- https://github.com/radix-ui/primitives/issues/2278
- https://www.radix-ui.com/primitives/docs/components/tooltip
- https://www.radix-ui.com/primitives/docs/components/hover-card
- https://github.com/shadcn-ui/ui/issues/2402

## Global search in top nav — width, placement, and coexistence with a Cmd-K command palette (SOC triage console)


**Key findings:**
- The dominant modern SaaS-console pattern (Linear, Vercel, GitHub, Retool, Superhuman, VS Code's cmdk) is NOT a persistent visible text input in the header — it's a small compact BUTTON/pill (placeholder label like "Search" + a `⌘K` hint chip) that opens a centered MODAL ~36-42rem (576-672px) wide containing the real `<input>`. Our webui already implements exactly this: webui/src/soc/AppShell.tsx (a `Button variant="outline" size="sm"` with a `CommandIcon` + '⌘K' `<kbd>`) opens webui/src/soc/components/CommandPalette.tsx, a `DialogContent` at `sm:max-w-xl` (576px).
- NN/g's classic 'always show a persistent, empty type-in box, not a link/button' guidance (nngroup.com/articles/search-visible-and-simple — the '+91% search use' finding) was measured on anonymous content/marketing sites where search IS the primary first action. It does NOT transfer cleanly to an authenticated, rail-navigated operator console where the left nav is the primary wayfinding tool and search is a secondary, cross-cutting utility — which is exactly why the SaaS-console industry converged on the button+shortcut launcher instead of NN/g's box. NN/g's own follow-up ('Search Box vs. Navigation') frames it as coexistence, not replacement.
- Elastic's own history is the clearest cautionary tale for conflating 'jump to a page/object' search with 'build a query': Kibana's GLOBAL nav search (Ctrl+/ / Cmd+/, per elastic Kibana docs) is a small icon/flyout for finding apps and saved objects — a completely separate UI element from the Discover/Security KQL query bar. When Kibana's Security-app KQL bar was narrowed in an 8.x redesign, security analysts filed elastic/kibana#36140 specifically because 'long and/or compound searches used by security analytics and threat hunting use cases need a wider/larger input field,' asking for permanent widening or a resizable/two-line field. Lesson: a compact ⌘K jump box and a full-width, growable log/query bar have fundamentally different width requirements and must stay separate UI elements.
- GitHub's evolution (github.blog/changelog/2018-05-24-jump-to) shows a persistent-looking top search field that behaves as a lightweight 'jump to' on click or the `/` key, later layered with a fuller Cmd+K Command Palette (docs.github.com) for scoped actions — reinforcing `/` as a well-established SECONDARY trigger key for focusing search, alongside Cmd/Ctrl+K, across dev-tool-adjacent products (GitHub, Slack, and Kibana's Ctrl+/ all use a slash-family shortcut).
- Sam Solomon's widely-cited 'Designing Command Palettes' (solomon.io) gives an explicit decision rule: if search is an important, standalone feature of the product, position search and the command palette SIDE-BY-SIDE at the same UI level (his cited example: Salesloft); otherwise fold search in as just one more action inside the palette. Retool's own design postmortem (retool.com/blog/designing-the-command-palette) shows they deliberately REJECTED scattering local search inputs throughout the app in favor of ONE palette with (a) a 'Top result' section so good matches aren't buried under category headings, and (b) a scoping model where removing a context 'pill' moves you back up a level — both directly reusable ideas.
- Microsoft's unified Defender portal (learn.microsoft.com) runs a persistent, portal-wide global search bar (not a button) precisely because there search is doing PRIMARY-task work: it's the one way to jump across two previously separate consoles' (Sentinel + XDR) entities, nav items, and docs at once. That is the actual signal for when a persistent input beats a button: when search is unifying disjoint surfaces the user has no other map of — not our situation today (one product, one left rail), but a relevant trigger if TLSOC's webui ever needs to bridge into an attached legacy Kibana/ELK surface again.
- Our existing implementation already gets the hard part right: CommandPalette.tsx groups results (Recent -> Cases -> Sources -> Actions -> Settings -> Nav), disables cmdk's local fuzzy filter for remote hits (`shouldFilter={false}`) so the server's ranking is authoritative, and renders every case/source title as plain text per the #9 untrusted-fencing rule. Separately, Cases.tsx already has its OWN page-scoped, flex-width filter input (`min-w-[16rem] flex-1`, line ~1088-1099) that is distinct from the Cmd-K palette — i.e., the two-tier 'compact jump box' + 'wide page-level filter' architecture the research above argues for is already in place; it just has a few rough edges (below).

**Concrete recommendations for us:**
- Keep the current button+⌘K-chip launcher pattern in AppShell.tsx as-is — do not swap it for a persistent always-visible header input; that would burn header width for no measured benefit in a rail-navigated console (see NN/g caveat above).
- Fix a live gap: the trigger button is `className="hidden h-8 gap-1.5 text-muted-foreground sm:inline-flex"` (AppShell.tsx ~line 547) — below the `sm` breakpoint (640px) it disappears with NO fallback, so a narrow-window/touch user has no visible way to open search (no physical Cmd/Ctrl+K). Add an icon-only `Button variant="ghost" size="icon"` fallback for `<sm` widths (aria-label="Search", same `onClick={() => setPaletteOpen(true)}`) — mirrors the NN/g-endorsed mobile pattern of a magnifying-glass-only affordance.
- Add `/` as a secondary open-shortcut in the existing keydown handler in AppShell.tsx (~lines 448-462), guarded so it only fires when `document.activeElement` is not an `<input>`/`<textarea>`/`[contenteditable]` — a 5-10 line change matching the GitHub/Slack/Kibana (`Ctrl+/`) convention analysts already know; surface it in the button's `aria-keyshortcuts` and the tooltip text ('Press / or ⌘K').
- Keep `DialogContent` at `sm:max-w-xl` (576px) for the palette — that's the right band vs. comparable launchers (VS Code ~600-680px, Spotlight ~600px). Do NOT widen it to accommodate log/query text. If/when the Round-4 unified `GET /api/logs` scatter-gather viewer gets a UI, give it its OWN dedicated, generously wide (ideally resizable or 2-line) filter input on its own page — this is the exact fix elastic/kibana#36140 asked for and Kibana's Security app never fully shipped; we can do better than Kibana here from day one instead of cramming query-building into the ⌘K box.
- Add a 'View all N results for "{query}"' footer row in CommandPalette.tsx when case/source hits are truncated — `api.search(term, 20)` (api.ts ~line 850) has a hard cap with no escape hatch today. Wire that footer item to `onNavigate('cases', {...})` pre-seeding the existing page-level search filter in Cases.tsx (the `filters.search` state feeding the `min-w-[16rem] flex-1` input at ~line 1093) so a truncated palette search doesn't feel like a dead end.
- Formally preserve and document the current two-tier search architecture as the standing pattern (good candidate for docs/research or DESIGN_STANDARD.md): Cmd-K palette = jump/lookup only (debounced ≥2 chars, `shouldFilter=false` server-ranked, plain-text #9-rendered, capped result count) vs. page-scoped flex-width filter inputs (Cases.tsx-style) = the tool for filtering within a data table/page. When new registry-driven pages ship (Rules, Dashboards, Baseline, Batch, Campaigns), reuse this split rather than inventing a third search idiom, and keep indexing new pages into the palette via the existing RBAC-filtered `navGroups`/`settingsTargets` pattern (never leak a link to a page the operator's role can't open).
- Low priority / only if result volume grows enough to feel noisy: add lightweight scope prefixes to the palette query (`case:`, `source:`), styled after Slack's `in:` qualifiers and Retool's scope-pill model, so power users can disambiguate without more visual width.

**Pitfalls to avoid:**
- Applying NN/g's 'always-visible persistent search box' literally to an authenticated console dashboard — that guidance was measured on public/content sites where search is the primary first action for an anonymous visitor, not a nav-first power tool; doing so burns header real estate without a matching usage increase.
- Reusing the compact Cmd-K jump box as a general log/query search bar — Elastic's own SIEM app drew a filed community complaint (elastic/kibana#36140) precisely because a narrowed 'everyday' query field couldn't hold compound security queries. Keep the two experiences, and their widths, separate.
- Hiding the search affordance behind a keyboard shortcut ALONE below the responsive breakpoint — a live gap in the current AppShell.tsx (`hidden ... sm:inline-flex` with no icon fallback) that strands narrow-window/touch users with no way in.
- Letting a global search silently truncate with no 'view more' path — feels broken once case/source volume grows past a debug-time hard cap (currently 20 in `api.search`).
- Widening the launcher dialog to 'fit more' instead of adding grouping/scoping/a view-all link — per Retool's postmortem, width isn't the axis that fixes discoverability past a certain result volume; grouping (already done) plus an escape hatch to the full page is.

**Sources:**
- https://www.nngroup.com/articles/search-visible-and-simple/
- https://www.nngroup.com/videos/search-box-vs-navigation/
- https://github.com/elastic/kibana/issues/36140
- https://www.elastic.co/guide/en/kibana/8.19/introduction.html
- https://github.blog/changelog/2018-05-24-jump-to/
- https://docs.github.com/en/get-started/accessibility/github-command-palette
- https://solomon.io/designing-command-palettes/
- https://retool.com/blog/designing-the-command-palette
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/sentinel/microsoft-sentinel-defender-portal
- https://blog.superhuman.com/how-to-build-a-remarkable-command-palette/
- https://ui.shadcn.com/docs/components/radix/command

## Empty states, loading skeletons, and error states for dashboards/case views — keeping a data-sparse first-run install feeling intentional, not broken (Round-7 TLSOC UI overhaul)


**Key findings:**
- Skeleton screens are perceived as 9-12% faster than spinners even at identical actual load time and can cut bounce rate 9-20% (Nielsen Norman Group). Timing rule of thumb (NN/g + shimmer-library guidance): show no indicator under ~300ms (avoids a jarring flash); use a spinner for 2-10s single-module/async actions; use a skeleton for full-page/section loads (up to ~10s); use a determinate progress bar only when a wait can exceed ~10s and duration is estimable.
- A skeleton only works if it mirrors the real layout's geometry — thick bar = title, thin bars = body copy, square block = image/icon/KPI-tile, matching line-count and card height. A generic shimmer block, or a 'frame-only' skeleton (chrome with no content placeholders), fails to build the anticipatory mental model and, per NN/g, makes users assume the page is broken if the wait drags on.
- Accessibility for loading UI is commonly done wrong: aria-busy="true" alone is not reliably honored by many screen readers (Adrian Roselli), so it should be paired with an explicit visually-hidden role="status" aria-live="polite" announcement; skeleton placeholders themselves should carry aria-hidden="true"; never stack aria-busy + role="alert" + aria-live="polite" on one region (called out repeatedly as contradictory 'ARIA abuse'); shimmer must hit 3:1 non-text contrast (WCAG 1.4.11) and must be neutralized under prefers-reduced-motion.
- Design systems (IBM Carbon, GitHub Primer/Blankslate, Pencil & Paper's empty-state taxonomy) converge on 3 empty-state categories needing DIFFERENT tone/iconography: (1) feature-never-used/first-run — onboarding-flavored copy, illustration OK, exactly one primary CTA; (2) temporarily/legitimately empty (filtered, cleared, 'inbox zero') — informational or even celebratory, must not read as broken; (3) error/degraded — muted alert icon (never playful/mascot art), plain-language explanation, direct recovery action. The anatomy of a good empty state is icon + primary copy (what belongs here) + secondary copy (why it's empty / what to do) + exactly one primary action, and that action's position should stay fixed whether the container is populated or empty so layout doesn't jump on first load.
- GitHub Primer's 'degraded experiences' pattern is the clearest treatment of partial/dashboard failure: don't conceal that something is wrong, but scope the blast radius — a global outage gets one banner at the top; a single broken widget gets a small muted inline error + alert icon (not full destructive-red treatment) so the rest of the page doesn't look broken; never silently remove content essential to a workflow (ambiguous 'was this deleted or just broken?'); never disable an actionable control purely because a secondary data fetch failed (an accessibility/workflow regression) — hide count badges/activity indicators instead of showing stale or wrong numbers.
- Good error copy answers 3 questions in plain language: what happened (never a stack trace or raw HTTP status/code), what can I do right now (retry / adjust / contact), and how do I get back to a working state — translate every technical error ("ECONNREFUSED", "500") into something an analyst without dev background can act on.
- The 'cold start' problem (zero-data-feels-broken) is a named, well-studied SaaS onboarding failure mode. Leading analytics/observability tools (Mixpanel and peers) solve it by offering a fully pre-populated, unmistakably-labeled demo/sandbox dataset a new user can explore before connecting real infrastructure, rather than a bare empty grid — this removes the 'is this even working' doubt entirely instead of just softening it with better copy.
- For a noise-reduction / auto-triage product specifically, 'zero open cases' is frequently a GOOD state (the funnel worked), not an absence of data — SOC dashboard design literature explicitly warns that lean, low-alert-count displays are the desired end state, not a failure signal, so generic 'nothing here' empty-state treatment is the wrong signal to send when the true cause is successful auto-closure rather than mis-configuration or an ingest failure.
- AI-trust / provenance research converges on: raw numeric confidence scores are misread by users as 'percent correct' (rarely the intended meaning) and should be paired with qualitative language or a link to supporting evidence; every AI-asserted claim should carry a clickable provenance trail back to the evidence/tool calls that produced it; fields the model is 'sure' about should be visually distinguished from fields it inferred/guessed; and the single most trust-destroying anti-pattern is 'confident-wrong' output — an AI-derived assessment rendered with the same visual authority (typography, color, position) as a raw asserted fact.
- Agent-observability tooling built for LangGraph-style ReAct agents (LangSmith and similar) renders the trace as a literal chronological Thought → Action → Observation sequence nested Chain → Tool → LLM-call, with a distinct prompt/response inspector — i.e., best-in-class agent UIs show the interrogation that produced a verdict, not just the verdict, which is the direct analogue of a 'story view' that must separate SIEM-asserted fact from AI-derived assessment.

**Concrete recommendations for us:**
- webui/src/soc/pages/Overview.tsx currently collapses two very different situations into one boolean: `const empty = !loading && !error && cases.length === 0 && !metrics?.total_cases;` renders the SAME 'No triage activity yet' / 'Connect a source' EmptyState whether zero sources are configured OR sources are configured and the pipeline is simply quiet. Split this into 3 derived states using the already-built `/api/sources/health` + `metrics`: (a) 0 sources configured → keep today's first-run copy+CTA; (b) ≥1 source connected AND poller has run AND 0 open cases → a NEW 'quiet/healthy' EmptyState variant (see next item) with no 'Connect a source' CTA; (c) a source is erroring/unauthenticated → route to LoadError/a degraded banner instead of EmptyState at all.
- Add a `variant="success"` to webui/src/soc/components/EmptyState.tsx (it currently only supports 'default' | 'error', with 'error' tinting the icon chip `critical`). Give 'success' a `success`/`text-success` tinted chip and use it for the 'quiet dashboard' case: icon=CheckCircle2, title e.g. 'All clear — nothing needs your attention', description pulling the count of alerts auto-closed as noise in the current time window (already computed for metrics/case_manager audit) so zero cases reads as a demonstrated result of the noise-reduction pipeline, not an ambiguous blank — directly implements the informational/celebratory empty-state split from Carbon/Primer/Pencil & Paper rather than treating every '0' the same as the first-run state.
- Since Demo Mode already exists end-to-end (api.demo.enable/reset/disable, DemoBanner.tsx, engine/demo_generator.py seeded OCSF data), wire a secondary action into the zero-sources-configured EmptyState: alongside the existing primary 'Connect a source' button, add a secondary 'Try demo data' button calling `api.demo.enable()` inline. This mirrors the Mixpanel-style cold-start fix (pre-populated sandbox beats a bare empty grid) and the product already has every backend piece needed — it's currently only reachable via the wizard, not from the dashboard's own empty state.
- Give KpiTile (webui/src/soc/components/*) a `status="unavailable"` rendering path (muted em-dash + small warning glyph + tooltip) and audit Overview.tsx / Metrics.tsx so each independent data source (metrics, campaigns, baseline, sources/health) is fetched and error-boundaried separately rather than behind one page-level `error` gate — this is the concrete Tailwind/React implementation of Primer's 'degraded experiences' guidance: one flaky connector or slow baseline call should mute a single tile, never blank the whole Security Command Center.
- Extend webui/src/ui/skeleton.tsx with a `SkeletonTable` that mirrors DataTable's actual column widths/row height (current SkeletonCard with `lines={6}` is a generic tall card, not a column-shaped preview) and use it for Cases.tsx / Sources.tsx list-view loading — a skeleton that doesn't preview the real column structure loses most of the priming benefit that is the whole reason to prefer skeletons over spinners.
- Add (or confirm) a shared `useDelayedLoading(~250-300ms)` gate before any Skeleton/PageSkeleton mounts, so cache hits and demo-mode's synthetic-instant responses never flash shimmer for a sub-300ms response — check existing call sites like Overview.tsx's `if (loading && !cases.length && !metrics)` for this guard specifically.
- Harden the loading-announcement path: PageSkeleton.tsx already sets `aria-busy="true" aria-label="Loading page"` on its container and SkeletonCard uses `aria-hidden` (both correct) — add one shared visually-hidden `<span role="status" aria-live="polite">Loading…</span>` per page-level loading boundary rather than relying on aria-busy alone, since research shows many screen readers don't reliably announce aria-busy by itself. Also audit that no surface combines aria-busy + role="alert" + aria-live="polite" simultaneously — EmptyState's `variant="error"` correctly sets `role="alert"`, but confirm it is never applied to a persistent loading/skeleton region (that combination is the specific anti-pattern flagged by accessibility research).
- Audit LoadError.tsx call sites (it already coerces via errorMessage() to plain text, correctly avoiding raw stack traces — good baseline) for two gaps: (1) every `title` prop reads as analyst-plain-language ('Could not load the dashboard') rather than backend jargon, and (2) every non-trivial data-fetching surface actually wires `onRetry` — grep for LoadError usages missing it, since an error with no retry path is a dead end the empty-state/error-state literature explicitly warns against.
- For the case 'story' view: extend the pattern already used in WhyPanel.tsx (agent-derived text rendered via CodeBlock/plain text per non-negotiable #9) into an explicit two-lane layout — a fact lane (OCSF-asserted fields straight from raw_data/unmapped, each tagged `<Badge variant="outline">SIEM</Badge>`) versus an assessment lane (verdict/confidence/reasoning, each tagged with the existing VerdictBadge/ConfidenceBadge PLUS a 'based on N tool calls' link into StageTimeline/TraceTimeline). This operationalizes the 'mark AI-sure vs AI-guessed differently' finding and stops AI-derived text from ever rendering with the same visual authority as a raw log field — the badge/icon distinction (not just color) also satisfies the WCAG non-color-only-signaling bar the codebase already applies via SEMANTIC_ICON elsewhere.
- Give StageTimeline/TraceTimeline the same 3-state discipline WhyPanel.tsx already has (it has a proper 'No rationale recorded yet' EmptyState) — confirm the timeline itself has an equivalent empty state (no trace yet = investigation pending, not a bug) and that a single failed tool-call step renders as one degraded row inline rather than silently truncating or hiding the rest of the trace, per the Primer degraded-experiences guidance applied to a list of steps instead of a list of widgets.
- Where a page/widget's only data is demo-seeded, tag its own EmptyState/KpiTile sub-copy with '(sample data)' in addition to the global DemoBanner — the banner can scroll out of view or be collapsed (DemoBanner.tsx supports a collapsed pill state persisted in localStorage), so any surface reachable without re-seeing the banner needs its own inline reminder to uphold the Round-2 guarantee that demo state must be 'loud, deliberate' and never confusable with production truth.
- Before shipping the new 'Security Command Center' KPI row and the 'Noise Reduction' funnel infographic, build their PageSkeleton-equivalent FIRST with matching tile/column heights (following the exact discipline already used in PageSkeleton.tsx, which mirrors PageHeader's icon-chip size and gap so the header doesn't visibly snap on resolve) — this prevents the new widgets from reintroducing cumulative-layout-shift regressions the rest of the app already guards against, and gate any funnel-diagram entrance animation through the same global prefers-reduced-motion CSS rule the shimmer already uses rather than a bespoke animation path.

**Pitfalls to avoid:**
- Treating 'zero cases' as always bad/broken. For a product whose entire value proposition is suppressing noise, a stark '0' or a generic 'nothing here' empty state reads as failure when it may be the system working exactly as intended — it must be visually disambiguated from 'not configured yet' and from 'pipeline is broken'.
- Full-page blocking spinners/skeletons for what is really several independently-loading widgets. One slow enrichment or baseline call should not blank the entire Command Center — gate loading/error per-widget, not per-page.
- Skeletons that don't mirror the final layout (generic bars/blocks instead of column-shaped or KPI-tile-shaped placeholders). This causes layout shift on resolve and forfeits the anticipatory-mental-model benefit that is the whole reason to prefer skeletons over spinners.
- Overusing or over-animating shimmer — fast/flashy sweeps read as jittery, and applying any loading indicator to responses that resolve in well under ~300ms (cache hits, demo-mode instant data) creates an annoying flash with no benefit.
- Combining aria-busy + role="alert" + aria-live="polite" on the same region — explicitly flagged by accessibility researchers as contradictory ARIA that confuses screen-reader users; pick one clear semantic per region.
- Relying on aria-busy alone to announce a loading state — many screen readers don't reliably honor it; it needs a paired explicit status announcement.
- Leaking raw backend/HTTP error text (stack traces, "500", "ECONNREFUSED") into analyst-facing error copy instead of translating to what-happened / what-to-do / how-to-recover language.
- Silently hiding a broken widget with no failure indicator at all — reads ambiguously as either 'this was deleted' or invisible brokenness; always show a small muted inline failure marker instead of blank space.
- Disabling actionable controls purely because a secondary/background data fetch failed — a workflow and accessibility regression; an analyst should still be able to act on a case even if, say, its enrichment panel failed to load.
- Letting AI-derived text (reasoning, inferred verdict, confidence) render with the same visual weight/authority as a raw SIEM-asserted field. Literature repeatedly names 'confident-wrong' AI output presented identically to fact as the fastest way to destroy analyst trust — this is the central risk for the case 'story' view.
- Showing a bare numeric confidence percentage with no context or evidence link — users misread it as 'percent correct,' which is rarely the intended semantic.
- Using playful illustrations/mascots on error or degraded states. Design-system guidance (Carbon, Primer) is explicit that illustration/mascot treatment belongs to first-run/onboarding empty states only — error states need a muted alert icon and direct language, never whimsy.
- Letting sample/demo data blend visually with real data at the per-widget level. It must be tagged inline wherever it can be viewed without the persistent top banner in view (scrolled past, banner collapsed, deep-linked/screenshotted page) — not just announced once globally.

**Sources:**

- https://www.nngroup.com/videos/skeleton-screens-vs-progress-bars-vs-spinners/
- https://carbondesignsystem.com/patterns/empty-states-pattern/
- https://primer.style/product/ui-patterns/empty-states/
- https://primer.style/design/components/blankslate
- https://primer.style/product/ui-patterns/degraded-experiences/
- https://www.pencilandpaper.io/articles/empty-states
- https://adrianroselli.com/2020/11/more-accessible-skeletons.html
- https://figr.design/blog/error-state-design-patterns
- https://www.2pointagency.com/glossary/error-state-design-patterns-a-comprehensive-guide/

## Accessibility for security dashboards (WCAG 2.2, accessible charts/funnels/gauges, keyboard nav for case tables, focus management in slide-over case detail) — applied to the TLSOC Round-7 Security Command Center / Noise-Reduction funnel / case-story view


**Key findings:**
- WCAG 2.2 (W3C Rec, Oct 2023, errata Dec 2024) adds 9 SC directly relevant here: 2.4.11 Focus Not Obscured (Minimum, AA) — a focused element must not be fully hidden behind sticky headers/footers/overlays; 2.4.13 Focus Appearance (AAA, stretch) — ring ≥2px perimeter + 3:1 contrast vs unfocused; 2.5.7 Dragging Movements (AA) — any drag interaction needs a non-drag keyboard alternative; 2.5.8 Target Size Minimum (AA) — interactive targets ≥24×24 CSS px, with a 'spacing' exception (24px offset to the nearest other target) and an 'equivalent control' exception. AA (not AAA) is the realistic, universally-cited commercial/legal conformance target (ADA Title II / EN 301549 / EAA all reference WCAG AA).
- Best-in-class chart accessibility (Highcharts a11y module, MIT Visualization Group research, Flourish, A11Y Collective) converges on three layers, not one: (1) a chart-level accessible name/summary (role=img + aria-label, or an SVG <title>), (2) either a keyboard-navigable drill-down mode OR a full visually-hidden <table> mirroring every datum, and (3) non-color encoding (icons/patterns/text labels) for every categorical distinction. Research is explicit that alt-text/aria-label ALONE ('a chart with N segments, total X') gives a gestalt takeaway but no way to recover specific numbers — screen-reader users need the underlying data, not just a description of the shape.
- ARIA has two distinct, frequently-confused range-widget roles: `meter` (a static value inside a known range — battery, disk usage, an alert-volume share, a risk score) vs `progressbar` (an ONGOING task moving toward completion, e.g. a file upload). The W3C APG is explicit that `meter` is the correct role for 'graphical display of a numeric value that varies within a defined range' and that `aria-valuetext` should be set whenever the raw valuenow%/valuemin/valuemax numbers alone wouldn't be user-friendly (e.g. 'battery: 50% (6 hours) remaining'). Per MDN/APG guidance, when both aria-valuenow and aria-valuetext are present, assistive tech announces the valuetext, not the number — so valuetext is exactly the mechanism for reconciling 'the bar's visual width is a % of max' with 'the printed number is a raw count.'
- For accessible modals/side-panels (W3C APG Dialog (Modal) Pattern + Radix Primitives docs), the required behaviors are: move focus INTO the dialog on open, trap it inside while open, close on Escape, RETURN focus to the exact triggering element on close, and give the dialog root an accessible name via `aria-labelledby` pointing at a real heading/Title element (a generic aria-label like 'dialog' or 'panel' is explicitly discouraged when the content has its own specific heading). APG also notes: if the first interactive element sits far down in a long/wide panel, autofocusing it can scroll the panel so its own heading/lede is out of view — the recommended fix is to give a static heading `tabindex="-1"` and focus THAT first, so the reading position starts at the top and is still keyboard-reachable.
- For row-based data tables (as opposed to true spreadsheet-like editable grids), the W3C APG draws a line between the full 'Grid' pattern (single roving tabindex, arrow-key virtual cursor, Page Up/Down for large datasets — appropriate when individual CELLS are independently interactive) and a simpler 'list of activatable rows' pattern (each row is one tab stop, Enter/Space activates it — appropriate when a whole row is one unit, e.g. 'open this case'). Real-world large-list UIs (Gmail-style inboxes, GitHub issue lists) commonly layer optional Up/Down arrow-key roving on top of the simple per-row-tab-stop model as a keyboard-power-user convenience, without adopting the heavier full-grid semantics — this avoids forcing a special 'grid navigation mode' on switch-device/voice-control users who rely on landing on any row directly.
- For custom dashboards with drag-to-move/drag-to-resize widgets (WCAG 2.5.7 Dragging Movements, AA), the accepted pattern is: keep the pointer-drag affordance, but give every draggable item a real, always-tabbable button/handle whose aria-label documents an arrow-key (move) / shift+arrow (resize) keyboard equivalent, PLUS an explicit menu with move-up/down and resize-wider/narrower/taller/shorter actions as a fully non-drag fallback — i.e., drag must never be the ONLY way to reach a given layout state.

**Concrete recommendations for us:**
- NoiseReductionFunnel (planned in docs/research/2026-07-round7/PLAN_v1.md ★b, webui/src/soc/components/NoiseFunnel.tsx, not yet built): give each severity/outcome segment `role="meter"` (NOT `role="progressbar"` — it's a static comparative value, not a running task) with `aria-valuemin=0`, `aria-valuemax={stageTotal}` (the real count, not a normalized 0–100), `aria-valuenow={segmentCount}`, and `aria-valuetext="342 critical alerts — 27% of 1,264 ingested"` so sighted users (who read the printed count) and screen-reader users (who hear valuetext) get the SAME number. Keep the planned sr-only `<table>` fallback (correct instinct) but also verify the meter's aria-label/valuetext alone is enough for someone who never opens the table — the table should be a supplement, not the only accessible surface.
- Before building NoiseFunnel, fix the same defect at its source: webui/src/soc/components/BarList.tsx line ~101 sets `role="progressbar" aria-valuenow={Math.round(pct)} aria-valuemin=0 aria-valuemax=100 aria-label={it.label}` — this announces a relative PERCENT-OF-MAX to screen readers while sighted users see `format(value)` (a raw count, e.g. '340'), and it uses the 'ongoing task' role for a static comparison. Since the Round-7 plan explicitly says NoiseFunnel will 'match that language exactly' (BarList/MitreHeatmap precedent), fixing BarList first prevents propagating the mismatch into the new component. Fix: swap to `role="meter"`, set `aria-valuemin=0 aria-valuemax={max}` (the real max, not 100), `aria-valuenow={value}` (the real value), and add `aria-valuetext={`${it.label}: ${format(value)}${showPercent ? ` (${Math.round(pct)}%)` : ''}`}`.
- CaseDetail.tsx's Sheet (webui/src/ui/sheet.tsx wraps @radix-ui/react-dialog): today `SheetContent` gets a generic `aria-label="Case detail"` and the visible case title is a plain `<h2>`, NOT Radix's `SheetPrimitive.Title` — so when the panel opens, assistive tech announces only 'Case detail, dialog', never which case (e.g. 'CASE-1042: Suspicious PowerShell activity'). Fix: render the `<h2>` through `SheetTitle` (already exported from ui/sheet.tsx) so Radix wires `aria-labelledby` automatically, and drop the generic `aria-label` override.
- Same Sheet: there are ZERO uses of Radix's `onOpenAutoFocus`/`onCloseAutoFocus` anywhere in webui/src (grep confirmed) — every Sheet/Dialog (CaseDetail, SourceLogsSheet, UnifiedLogsSheet, RuleLifecycleSheet, WidgetConfigSheet) relies on Radix's default 'focus the first tabbable element,' which in CaseDetail lands on an icon-only header action button (Reinvestigate) rather than the title or the tab list — a surprising first stop that a keyboard user could accidentally activate. Fix (CaseDetail.tsx around the header `<h2>`): give the title `tabIndex={-1}` and wire `onOpenAutoFocus={(e) => { e.preventDefault(); titleRef.current?.focus(); }}` on `SheetContent` so focus starts at the top of the reading order (per the APG Dialog example's guidance for long/wide panels), not on a random action icon 800px to the right.
- The planned InvestigationPanel (docs/research/2026-07-round7/PLAN_v1.md #9a) uses `SegmentedControl` (webui/src/soc/components/SegmentedControl.tsx, built on Radix RadioGroup) to switch between Story/Rationale/Trace bodies. SegmentedControl's own doc comment says it deliberately replaced Radix Tabs because it is meant for VALUE pickers with 'no owned panel' — but here it DOES own a panel (a large content region really is swapped). Recommend either: (a) give this specific switcher real tab semantics (`role="tablist"`/`role="tab"`/`role="tabpanel"` + `aria-controls`, e.g. via Radix Tabs) since content ownership is exactly the case Tabs solves, or (b), the low-risk fix if keeping SegmentedControl, call the app's existing `useAnnouncer()` (webui/src/soc/components/announcer.tsx — already wired into DataTable sort) on every view change, e.g. `announce('Showing Rationale view')`, and wrap the swapped panel in `<div role="region" aria-label="Investigation — Rationale">` with its own visible heading so a screen-reader user who continues reading past the control discovers the content changed.
- DataTable.tsx clickable rows (`tabIndex=0`, `role="button"` per row, Enter/Space to open) are a valid, simpler alternative to a full ARIA Grid and should NOT be rewritten into one. But for SOC triage tables running 50–100 rows per page, add an OPTIONAL keyboard convenience on top: `ArrowDown`/`ArrowUp` on a focused row moves focus to the adjacent row (`.focus()` on the sibling `<tr>`), `Home`/`End` jump to the first/last row on the page — purely additive, doesn't change the Tab-stop-per-row contract, and mirrors how large row-based inboxes (not full data grids) are made keyboard-fast in practice.
- ui/checkbox.tsx: the Radix Checkbox root is `h-4 w-4` (16×16 CSS px) with no padded hit-area wrapper, and DataTable.tsx's select-row/select-all checkbox cells only add padding to the surrounding `<TableCell>` (`px-4 py-3`), not to the checkbox's own interactive box. Under WCAG 2.2 SC 2.5.8 (AA) this needs auditing against the 'Spacing' exception (does a 24px circle centered on the checkbox avoid overlapping the checkbox in the row above/below?); if it doesn't clear that exception, bump the Checkbox root to `size-6` (24px) or wrap it in an invisible padded hit-area (`before:absolute before:-inset-1`), which is a one-line, low-risk fix given how small the component is.
- RiskGauge.tsx already does the right baseline thing (role="img" + a computed `<title>` carrying value+band, plus a non-color band-name label per §6.1) — good, keep as-is for AA. As a forward-looking (not urgent) refinement for the new top-right `RiskIndexBadge`/`ActiveRiskIndex` instrument (Round-7 item #1), consider promoting it to `role="meter"` with real `aria-valuemin=0 aria-valuemax=100 aria-valuenow={score}` plus `aria-valuetext` (keeping the `<title>` as a redundant fallback) so assistive tech that specifically supports range-widget value queries gets structured data, not just a name string.
- The custom-dashboard drag/resize surface (webui/src/soc/dashboard/WidgetGrid.tsx / EditableGrid.tsx) already correctly ships arrow-key move + shift+arrow resize + a non-drag move/resize menu per widget (confirmed in code — this satisfies WCAG 2.5.7 today). Treat this as the house pattern: if Round-7 introduces any NEW drag interaction (e.g., drag-to-reorder columns, drag-to-reorder dashboard widgets in a list), copy this exact arrow-key + explicit-menu approach rather than inventing a new one.
- Wire `usePrefersReducedMotion()` (already used elsewhere) into every Round-7 count-up/animated element — the NoiseFunnel headline %, stage-bar width transitions, and any new KPI count-up — so the final numbers render immediately with no animated ramp when reduced motion is requested; the plan already states this intent for NoiseFunnel, make sure it's applied consistently to any Overview/Command-Center count-up primitives too (item #5's motion system).
- Run `npx vitest run` with the existing `overview.a11y.test.tsx` / `widget-grid-a11y.test.tsx` jest-axe pattern against every NEW Round-7 surface (NoiseFunnel, InvestigationPanel, ProvenanceTag, ActiveRiskIndex/RiskIndexBadge) before merging — the codebase already has the harness; extend it rather than adding a new one.

**Pitfalls to avoid:**
- Do not use `role="progressbar"` for any static comparative value (severity share, risk score, alert-volume segment) — it semantically claims an operation is 'in progress,' and per APG the correct role for a bounded static value is `meter`. This is the exact defect already present in BarList.tsx and about to be replicated in the planned NoiseFunnel unless corrected first.
- Do not let `aria-valuenow`/`aria-valuemin`/`aria-valuemax` communicate a DIFFERENT number than what's printed on screen (e.g., valuenow=a normalized 0–100 percent while the visible label shows a raw count). Always add `aria-valuetext` to say exactly what the sighted user sees, or normalize both to the same unit.
- Do not rely on `aria-label="<generic name>"` on a dialog/sheet root when the content has its own specific, dynamic heading (a case title, a source name) — wire the real heading through the primitive's own Title component (`SheetPrimitive.Title` / `DialogPrimitive.Title`) so `aria-labelledby` tracks the actual content, otherwise every instance of that dialog announces identically and keyboard/screen-reader users can't tell which one opened.
- Do not assume Radix's default initial-focus ("focus the first tabbable element in DOM order") is fine for wide/action-heavy panels — verify what that first element actually is; if it's a destructive or state-changing icon button (Reinvestigate, Close-with-disposition, Remove), a keyboard user landing there and reflexively pressing Enter/Space could trigger it unintentionally.
- Do not convert a simple 'list of clickable rows' table into a full WAI-ARIA `role="grid"` just because 'tables should be grids' — the full grid pattern (roving tabindex, per-cell focus, arrow-key virtual cursor) is heavier, riskier to retrofit, and is the WRONG pattern when a whole row (not individual cells) is the unit of interaction; it can actually make things worse for screen-reader/switch users by forcing an unfamiliar navigation mode.
- Do not add ARIA `meter`/`progressbar`/`aria-live` announcements everywhere reflexively — over-announcing (e.g., a live region firing on every animation tick of a count-up) creates noise that makes screen-reader users start ignoring the region; keep announcements to discrete, meaningful state changes (sort changed, view switched, N rows selected), which is exactly the discipline the existing `useAnnouncer()`/DataTable pattern already follows — extend that discipline, don't dilute it.
- Do not ship a new drag-only interaction (e.g. drag-to-reorder anything) without copying the existing WidgetGrid.tsx arrow-key-move + explicit-menu pattern; per WCAG 2.5.7 (AA) drag can never be the ONLY way to reach a given state.
- Do not treat WCAG 2.2's AAA-level criteria (2.4.12 Focus Not Obscured Enhanced, 2.4.13 Focus Appearance) as blocking — they're stretch goals; the must-hold bar for a commercial SOC product is AA, which already covers 2.4.11 (Focus Not Obscured Minimum), 2.5.7 (Dragging), and 2.5.8 (Target Size).
